(t)

(t)

Figure 11:
Vector-scalar scattering (omitting the
u
-channel).

Unitarity implies that an amplitude with the correct
t
-channel factorisation structure has the form

\displaystyle A_{t}(W_{A},W_{B},\varphi_{i},\varphi_{j})

\displaystyle=(t_{A})_{im}(t_{B})_{mj}\frac{1}{t-m_{t_{m}}^{2}}\frac{1}{m_{1}m_{2}}\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{3}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right],

(176)

where
m_{t_{m}}
is the mass of exchanged particle
m
(this label is implicitly summed over). The
u
-channel amplitude is the same, but with the vector boson labels exchanged and obviously
t
and
m_{t_{m}}
replaced by
u
and
m_{u_{m}}
. The
s
-channel amplitude is

\displaystyle A_{s}(W_{A},W_{B},\varphi_{i},\varphi_{j})

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{-i}{4}\frac{1}{s-m_{s_{M}}^{2}}\frac{1}{m_{s_{M}}^{2}m_{1}m_{2}}(t_{M})_{ij}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\big(m_{s_{M}}^{2}{f}_{AB}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left(m_{1}\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{3}-p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]+m_{2}\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{3}-p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]+\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left(u-t\right)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+m_{s_{M}}^{2}({f}_{AB}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M})^{*}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left(m_{1}\left[\bf{1}\bf\right\rvert p_{3}-p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\right\rangle+m_{2}\left[\bf{2}\bf\right\rvert p_{3}-p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{1}\right\rangle+\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left(u-t\right)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\left(m_{1}{f}_{BM}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,A}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]+m_{2}({f}_{MA}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,B})^{*}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\left((m_{3}^{2}-m_{4}^{2})\left(m_{1}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle-m_{2}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\right)+m_{s_{M}}^{2}\left[\bf{1}\bf\right\rvert p_{3}-p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\left(m_{1}({f}_{BM}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,A})^{*}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle+m_{2}{f}_{MA}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,B}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\left((m_{3}^{2}-m_{4}^{2})\left(m_{1}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]-m_{2}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\right)+m_{s_{M}}^{2}\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{3}-p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]\right)\big).

(177)

The masses
m_{s_{M}}
are those of the exchanged vector bosons. The form presented here has manifest exchange symmetries.

These terms describing the factorisation channels all have high-energy scaling
\sim E^{2}
when the vectors both have longitudinal helicity, but weaker
\sim E
scaling for other helicity configurations. Contact terms for this amplitude have weakest high energy scaling
\sim E^{2}
. This is obvious from the fact that there is a unique, lowest dimension term with this scaling for each of three different helicity configurations. For longitudinal helicity, this term is

\displaystyle A_{c}(W_{A},W_{B},\varphi_{i},\varphi_{j})=\frac{c_{AB;ij}}{m_{1}m_{2}}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle

(178)

and it contributes to the amplitude at the same order in the HEL as the terms constructed above, so should be included. Here
c_{AB;ij}
is some coupling constant that is generally free, but also participates in any constraint arising from cancellation in the HEL.

Taking the high energy limit of the combined set of terms in the amplitude and demanding that they cancel implies that:

\displaystyle(t_{A})_{im}(t_{B})_{mj}t+(t_{B})_{im}(t_{A})_{mj}u+\frac{i}{2}\Re{f}_{AB}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M}(t_{M})_{ij}(u-t)-c_{AB;ij}s=0.

(179)

Equating to zero the coeffecients of the two independent Mandlestam variables fixes the contact term:

\displaystyle c_{AB;ij}=-\frac{1}{2}\left((t_{A})_{im}(t_{B})_{mj}+(t_{B})_{im}(t_{A})_{mj}\right)

(180)

and subsequently necessitates the constraint:

\displaystyle(t_{A})_{im}(t_{B})_{mj}-(t_{B})_{im}(t_{A})_{mj}=i\Re{f}_{AB}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M}(t_{M})_{ij}.

(181)

The scalar-vector couplings must therefore be identified with Lie algebra generators in some representation, accepting that the real parts of the vector self-couplings are structure constants of the Lie algebra. This interpretation is independent of the further conditions needed to unitarise four vector scattering below
\sim E^{2}
scaling, and is, in particular, not reliant upon assumptions about the imaginary parts of the vector self-couplings or the signature of the Lie algebra. However, since the generators are self-adjoint, any representation satisfying (
181
) must be infinite dimensional if the Lie algebra is not compact.

Implicit in “high-energy limit” is the assumption of a mass gap. The tests of unitarity employed here all assume this for the specific amplitudes under consideration. It is not immediately required that the entire underlying theory be consistent with this assumption, only the subset of particles participating in the tree-level amplitudes. This accords with the bootstrap philosophy of necessity instead of sufficiency. The requirement derived in (
181
) (and similarly all of the conditions on the vector boson amplitude derived in Section
4.2.1
above) must hold for a theory in which each of the amplitudes between mass eigenstates are gapped. This need not necessarily apply to the overarching theory in which these amplitudes exist (although this assumption would also be sufficient for these conclusions to hold). This is the reason that KK YM obeys the relations derived in the previous Section, in spite of not being gapped, as well as the reason that it is not perturbatively unitary in the HEL but still consistent with these results. Similarly, the partial unitarisation of these mixed scalar amplitudes was predicated upon the assumption of an upper bound on the participating particle masses, yet the conclusions seem to accommodate theories that require an infinite number of states. Such theories may nevertheless be consistent with the analysis provided that the mass eigenstate amplitudes are gapped. The possible impacts of a tower within the amplitudes will remain, as far as this analysis is concerned, an open question.

With the contact term fixed, the next task is to find a representation of the amplitude in which the diverging HEL terms are explicitly removed. The terms scaling as
\sim E^{2}
in the complete amplitude can be grouped into two structures:

\displaystyle A_{E^{2}}(W_{A},W_{B},\varphi_{i},\varphi_{j})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2m_{1}m_{2}}\left((t_{A})_{im}(t_{B})_{mj}+(t_{B})_{im}(t_{A})_{mj}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\left(\frac{\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{3}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]}{t-m_{t}^{2}}+\frac{\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{3}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]}{u-m_{u}^{2}}-\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{2m_{1}m_{2}}i\Re{f}_{AB}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M}(t_{M})_{ij}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\left(\frac{\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{3}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]}{t-m_{t}^{2}}-\frac{\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{3}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]}{u-m_{u}^{2}}+\frac{t-u}{s-m_{s}^{2}}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\right).

(182)

I have homogenised the masses in the propagators as in (
138
) and omitted the terms proportional to the mass splittings in each channel, although I will use these briefly further below. The kinematic structure accompanying the anticommutator of generators (the “Abelian” part of the amplitude) can be algebraically reduced in order to manifest a unitary HEL. Using identity (
A
), these terms can be combined as

\displaystyle\left(u-m_{u}^{2}\right)\left(t-m_{t}^{2}\right)\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle-\left(u-m_{u}^{2}\right)\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{3}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]-\left(t-m_{t}^{2}\right)\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{3}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]

\displaystyle=\left(m_{3}^{2}+m_{4}^{2}-m_{t}^{2}-m_{u}^{2}\right)\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]-m_{1}m_{2}\left(\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]^{2}+\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+m_{2}\left(m_{u}^{2}-m_{1}^{2}-m_{4}^{2}\right)\left(\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]+\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad-m_{1}\left(m_{t}^{2}-m_{2}^{2}-m_{4}^{2}\right)\left(\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]+\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\left((m_{1}^{2}+m_{2}^{2}-m_{3}^{2})m_{4}^{2}+m_{t}^{2}m_{u}^{2}-m_{1}^{2}m_{t}^{2}-m_{2}^{2}m_{u}^{2}+m_{1}^{2}m_{2}^{2}\right)\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad-m_{1}m_{2}m_{4}^{2}\left(\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]^{2}+\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle^{2}\right)

(183)

over a common denominator of
\frac{-1}{(t-m_{t}^{2})(u-m_{u}^{2})}
. This is now manifestly unitarised.

The remaining terms in (
4.3.1
) accompanying the factor of the structure constant can be likewise reduced. The terms involving explicit appearances of
t
can be combined as

\displaystyle t\left(t-m_{t}^{2}\right)\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle+\left(s-m_{s}^{2}\right)\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{3}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]

\displaystyle=-\left(t-m_{t}^{2}\right)\left(m_{1}\left(\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]+\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]\right)+m_{2}\left(\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]+\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]\right)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+(2m_{2}^{2}+m_{3}^{2}+m_{4}^{2}-m_{t}^{2}-m_{s}^{2})\left(t-m_{t}^{2}\right)\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad+\left(m_{s}^{2}-m_{1}^{2}-m_{2}^{2}\right)\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]-m_{1}m_{2}\left(\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]^{2}+\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad-m_{2}\left(m_{s}^{2}+m_{t}^{2}-m_{2}^{2}-m_{3}^{2}\right)\left(\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]+\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad-m_{1}\left(m_{t}^{2}-m_{2}^{2}-m_{4}^{2}\right)\left(\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]+\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\left(m_{t}^{2}(2m_{2}^{2}+m_{3}^{2}+m_{4}^{2}-m_{t}^{2}-m_{s}^{2})+m_{2}^{2}\left(m_{s}^{2}-m_{2}^{2}-m_{3}^{2}\right)+m_{4}^{2}\left(m_{1}^{2}-m_{3}^{2}\right)\right)\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad-m_{4}^{2}m_{1}m_{2}\left(\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]^{2}+\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle^{2}\right)

(184)

multiplied by
\frac{1}{(s-m_{s}^{2})(t-m_{t}^{2})}
. The other terms given by combining
s
and
u
pole terms are obtained by exchanging particles
1
and
2
.

This leaves divergent terms that scale as
\sim E
for a single transverse and longitudinally polarised vector. Contact terms with HEL scaling
\sim E
don’t exist, so unitarisation can only involve further constraints on the
3
-particle couplings. The remaining divergent terms in (
4.3.1
) directly cancel those in (
4.3.1
) provided that the couplings obey the relations:

\displaystyle\Re f_{AB}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M}(t_{M})_{ij}=f_{AB}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M}(t_{M})_{ij}=f_{BM}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,A}(t_{M})_{ij}=f_{MA}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,B}(t_{M})_{ij}.

(185)

Each adjoint index
A
denotes a linearly independent vector particle. Momentarily assuming that no other particle exchanges contribute to the amplitude, then the fact that the couplings
\{(t_{A})_{ij}\}
constitute a representation of the Lie algebra implies that they are linearly independent. This subsequently implies (
148
), which again is just a statement of the standard properties of the YM Lie algebra (i.e. that the structure constants are fully antisymmetric). Of course, this was necessary in the previous Section to unitarise the
4
-vector amplitude below
\sim E^{2}
HEL scaling in the first place, so could be reasonably regarded as an assumption here rather than a conclusion. This becomes the necessary point of view when the scalars are Higgs bosons in the calculations further below in Section
4.3.2
. The reason that these conditions only become necessary at
\sim E
order here is that, as is clear from the three vector amplitude in the unitarity basis (
4.1
), the emergent
c
-type couplings can only appear in this amplitude as single insertions. In the HEL, these
\sim E
terms match onto massless amplitudes with one external gluon and three scalars in which a gluon is exchanged between a dim-
5
coupling and the regular scalar-gluon coupling in (
12
).

The remaining terms in the amplitude after the final cancellation produced by (
185
) are fully unitarised. The left-over single
s
-pole terms combine to give

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2m_{1}m_{2}}if_{ABM}(t_{M})_{ij}\frac{1}{s-m_{s}^{2}}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left((m_{u}^{2}-m_{t}^{2})+\frac{1}{m_{s_{M}}^{2}}(m_{3}^{2}-m_{4}^{2})(m_{2}^{2}-m_{1}^{2})\right).

(186)

An
s
-channel exchange of a Higgs boson is consistent with unitarity in itself and does not modify the argument above. It can contribute a term

\displaystyle A_{h}(W_{A},W_{B},\varphi_{i},\varphi_{j})=\frac{C_{ijm}\lambda_{AB}^{m}}{s-m_{h_{m}}^{2}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle,

(187)

where
C_{ijm}
is a possible scalar trilinear coupling and
m_{h_{m}}
is the Higgs mass. In the high energy limit, this combination of coupling constants is reinterpreted as a quartic coupling between the scalars and the longitudinal modes of the vectors.

There still remains some algebraic rearrangement necessary to reconcile this amplitude with the corresponding massless amplitudes that are expected to emerge in the HEL. For both vectors transversely polarised, this is already manifest. The terms proportional to
\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]^{2}
and
\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]^{2}
obtained by combining (
4.3.1
) with (
4.3.1
) and (
4.3.1
) already clearly match onto the expected massless expressions for Compton scattering of gluons off a scalar. However, the case where the vectors are both longitudinally polarised requires more verification.

The longitudinal case involves two sets of terms in the HEL: those describing internal particle exchange and the contact terms (see Section
3.3
for discussion of this in the QED case). Firstly are the terms that retain a pole in the HEL. These are given by the first term in the third line of (
4.3.1
) (when substituted into (
4.3.1
)). However, there is also a further contribution from the mass splitting terms. These are given by the contributions from the leading-order terms in (
4.3.1
) to the mass splitting terms in (
138
), which clearly share the same coupling constant prefactors assuming (
148
). Combining together and reducing these two sets of terms requires another application of the spinor identity underpinning (
4.3.1
). I will not bother to present this here, but simply make note that the leading order
\sim E^{0}
terms are simply modified by having
m_{s}^{2}
replaced with
m_{s_{M}}^{2}
in (
4.3.1
). These then match onto the expected expression for gluon exchange between scalars in scalar QCD (as can be seen from taking the massless limit of (
255
) with equal mass scalars in Appendix
D.1
). Here, the emergent generator (
106
) appears describing the coupling of the internal vector to the two emergent longitudinal scalars.

This leaves the terms that produce a quartic contact interaction between scalars in the HEL. The scalar quartic amplitude so obtained is

\displaystyle A_{c}(W_{A}^{L},W_{B}^{L},\varphi_{i},\varphi_{j})

\displaystyle\rightarrow\frac{\sqrt{2}}{4}C_{ijm}\lambda_{AB}^{m}+\frac{i}{4m_{1}m_{2}}f_{ABM}(t_{M})_{ij}\frac{1}{m_{s_{M}}^{2}}(m_{3}^{2}-m_{4}^{2})(m_{2}^{2}-m_{1}^{2})

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{4m_{1}m_{2}}\big((m_{3}^{2}+m_{4}^{2})\left((t_{A})_{im}(t_{B})_{mj}+(t_{B})_{im}(t_{A})_{mj}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-2m_{t_{m}}^{2}(t_{A})_{im}(t_{B})_{mj}-2m_{u_{m}}^{2}(t_{B})_{im}(t_{A})_{mj}\big).

(188)

The terms in the last line are supplied by the mass splitting terms canceling the internal mass terms appearing in the first line of (
4.3.1
) and the single-pole terms (
186
). This cancellation can be made manifest in the massive amplitude by applying (
A
) to the latter pair of terms, although I will not bother to present the reduced result here.

In order to complete the two massive vector, two scalar amplitude, possible contributions from
t
and
u
-channel vector boson exchange remain to be included. Assuming that the scalar-vector coupling is restricted to the Higgs-like coupling in (
98
), then the
t
-channel vector exchange can be calculated as

\displaystyle A_{t}(W_{A},W_{B},\varphi_{i},\varphi_{j})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{t-m_{t_{M}}^{2}}\frac{1}{4}\lambda^{i}_{AM}\lambda^{j}_{MB}\left(\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{3}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]-2m_{t_{M}}^{2}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\right).

(189)

The
u
-channel term is analogous. The second term in (
189
) is manifestly unitary. The first has an identical form to (
176
), with the identification of
\lambda^{i}_{AM}=-2i(t_{A})_{Mi}/m_{1}
and
\lambda^{j}_{MB}=-2i(t_{B})_{Mj}/m_{2}
, in agreement with the requirement from the study of the Higgs couplings in vector boson scattering above. Including this vector exchange term therefore modifies the unitarity constraints (
181
) by simply extending the sum in the matrix product of generators to include the intermediate components indexed by vector colour
M
, while the kinematics otherwise algebraically reduces in exactly the same way. The intermediate states that provide the representation space for the generators can therefore be a mix of independent matter scalars (Higgs bosons) and longitudinal modes from the massive vectors. As long as they arrange into a consistent representation, they can collectively unitarise the amplitude (more precisely, it has at this point been established that the commutator holds when both external coordinates are identified either with external scalars or longitudinal vectors - the case in which they are one of each type remains to be derived in the next Section below). The new couplings also modify the unitarised amplitudes in (
4.3.1
) by similarly extending the sum over internal generator indices appearing in the expression. However, the kinematically new term proportional to the internal mass in (
189
) matches onto a new
t
-channel gluon exchange between massless scalars in the HEL. The generators supplied by the Higgs couplings match onto the generators describing the couplings of the exchanged gluon to Higgs bosons and the emergent longitudinal scalars. Finally, contributions from the “holomorphic” terms in (
98
) are calculated in Appendix
D.3
. They clearly give contributions to the amplitude that have
\sim E^{2}
HEL divergences (for opposite transverse helicities) and can only be eliminated by switching them off. This justifies ignoring them here.

Note that at least two separate channels are required for this amplitude to unitarise. In particular, the
s
-channel exchange of a vector boson must be accompanied by a cross-channel exchange of scalar, possibly the longitudinal mode of another massive vector, that Higgses the amplitude. This remains the case if the
s
-channel vector is massless, as in the case of electromagnetic
W
-boson scattering. This parallels the inescapable generation of cross-channel Mandelstam poles in the residues of massless gluon amplitudes.

The results of the calculations can be checked against the elegant predictions from
\mathcal{N}=4
SYM. The mixed vector-scalar
3
-particle component amplitudes can be extracted from the
3
-particle superamplitude (
75
). For most of the scalars, these couplings are simply minimal. However, the minimal coupling of
\phi_{(12)}
in (
66
) is accompanied by a mixing angle
A[\phi_{(12)},\phi_{(12)},W_{A}]\propto\cos\left(\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{2}\right)
, while this scalar also has a further Higgs interaction with coupling
A[\phi_{(12)},W_{A},W_{B}]\propto\frac{1}{m_{1}}\sin\left(\varphi_{2}-\varphi_{3}\right)
. Note that the superamplitude with one massless and two massive vector multiplets always contains a Higgs coupling involving the massless
\phi_{[12]}
particle. Higgs bosons are always present in massless multiplets in the theory, but need not exist in massive multiplets unless central charges are misaligned.

With the dependence of the coupling constants on the central charges established, the
4
-particle amplitudes constructed out of
\mathcal{N}=4
SYM
3
-particle component amplitudes can be compared to the expected result directly extracted from the
4
-particle superamplitude. The four vector superamplitude for a particular colour ordering with
s
and
u
poles was exhibited in (
76
), from which the mixed scalar-vector components can be extracted. Taking the scalars to be those in (
66
) corresponding to minimum and maximum Grassmann degree respectively, the amplitude is

\displaystyle A[W,W,\phi,\widetilde{\phi}]

\displaystyle=\left(m_{4}\left(\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle e^{2i\varphi_{4}}-\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]e^{i(\varphi_{1}+\varphi_{2})}\right)+e^{i\varphi_{4}}\left(e^{i\varphi_{2}}\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]-e^{i\varphi_{1}}\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]\right)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\left(m_{4}\left(\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle e^{-2i\varphi_{4}}-\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]e^{-i(\varphi_{1}+\varphi_{2})}\right)+e^{-i\varphi_{4}}\left(e^{-i\varphi_{2}}\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]-e^{-i\varphi_{1}}\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]\right)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\frac{-1}{(s-m_{s}^{2})(u-m_{u}^{2})}.

(190)

The numerator is identical for the other channel pairings. The choice of external particles preserves the factorised form of the numerator as a product of numerators from
\mathcal{N}=2
amplitudes:
N_{\mathcal{N}=2}[\chi,\chi,\phi,\widetilde{\phi}]\times N_{\mathcal{N}=2}[\chi,\chi,\phi,\widetilde{\phi}]
, where
N_{\mathcal{N}=2}
denotes the numerator of the appropriate component amplitude contained (
78
). When the external scalars are both identified as
\phi_{(12)}
, then (
4.3.1
) has a further overall factor of
\cos(\varphi_{3}-\varphi_{4})
. Comparing this to (
4.3.1
) and (
4.3.1
) after applying the BPS conditions to the masses gives agreement. With aligned central charges, the single pole term (
186
) vanishes, leaving only terms with two separate Mandelstam poles, as expected from (
4.3.1
). When the central charges are misaligned, the single
s
-pole term (
186
) is non-zero but is canceled by an
s
-channel Higgs exchange instead.

4.3.2
Three vectors and one scalar

The unitarisation of Higgs-vector scattering therefore establishes another block of the commutation relations of the Lie algebra involving the generators describing the Higgs-vector couplings. The final components of this matrix relation must be derived from unitarising the three vector, one scalar amplitude.

All three factorisation channels for this amplitude are related by vector boson exchange symmetries.

Figure 12:
s
-channel contributions for scattering of three vectors and a scalar.

The
s
-channel term arising from scalar exchange is

\displaystyle A_{s\varphi}(W_{A},W_{B},W_{C},\varphi_{i})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}m_{3}}\lambda^{m}_{AB}(t_{C})_{mi}\frac{1}{s-m_{s_{m}}^{2}}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{3}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{3}\bf\right],

(191)

where the exchanged scalar must also be a Higgs boson. The analogous contribution arising from vector boson exchange is

\displaystyle A_{sW}(W_{A},W_{B},W_{C},\varphi_{i})

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{i}{4\sqrt{2}}\frac{1}{s-m_{s_{M}}^{2}}\frac{m_{s_{M}}}{m_{1}m_{2}}\lambda^{i}_{CM}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\Bigg(\Re{f}_{AB}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M}(\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left(\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{2}\bf\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{3}\right\rangle+\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left[\bf{1}\bf\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{3}\right\rangle-2\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left(\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{3}\bf\right]+\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{3}\bf\right]\right)-2\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle))

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+2m_{1}\Big(\left(i\Im{f}_{AB}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M}-{f}_{BM}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,A}\right)\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\left(i\Im{f}_{AB}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M}-({f}_{BM}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,A})^{*}\right)\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+2m_{2}\Big(\left(i\Im{f}_{AB}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M}-{f}_{MA}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,B}\right)\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\left(i\Im{f}_{AB}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M}-({f}_{MA}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,B})^{*}\right)\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\quad+\left(m_{1}{f}_{BM}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,A}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]+m_{2}({f}_{MA}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,B})^{*}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\qquad\quad\times\left(-2\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]+\frac{1}{m_{s_{M}}^{2}}\left(m_{1}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle-m_{2}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\right)\left\langle\bf{3}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{3}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\quad+\left(m_{1}({f}_{BM}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,A})^{*}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle+m_{2}{f}_{MA}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,B}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\quad\qquad\times\left(-2\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle+\frac{1}{m_{s_{M}}^{2}}\left(m_{1}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]-m_{2}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\bf{3}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{3}\bf\right]\right)\Bigg).

(192)

The
t
-channel terms are obtained by exchanging
2\leftrightarrow 3
, while the
u
-channel is given by
1\leftrightarrow 3
.

Insertions of the
c
-type terms in (
98
) give contributions to the
4
-leg amplitude that I separately present as

\displaystyle A_{\varphi F^{2}}(W_{A},W_{B},W_{C},\varphi_{i})

\displaystyle=\frac{-1}{s-m_{s_{m}}^{2}}\frac{1}{m_{3}}(t_{C})_{im}\left\langle\bf{3}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{3}\bf\right]\left(\frac{c^{m}_{AB}}{\Lambda}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]^{2}+\frac{(c^{m}_{AB})^{*}}{\Lambda}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{s-m_{s_{M}}^{2}}\frac{i}{2m_{1}m_{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\Bigg(m_{s_{M}}^{2}\left(f_{AB}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M}+(f_{AB}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M})^{*}\right)\left(\frac{c_{CM}^{i}}{\Lambda}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]-\frac{(c_{CM}^{i})^{*}}{\Lambda}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{c_{CM}^{i}}{\Lambda}\bigg(\left(f_{BM}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,A}+(f_{AB}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M})^{*}\right)m_{1}\left(\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left(m_{1}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle-m_{2}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\right)-\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]^{2}\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\left(f_{MA}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,B}+(f_{AB}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M})^{*}\right)m_{2}\left(\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left(m_{2}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle-m_{1}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\right)+\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]^{2}\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{2}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\left((f_{BM}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,A})^{*}-(f_{AB}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M})^{*}\right)m_{1}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{3}\bf\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\left((f_{MA}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,B})^{*}-(f_{AB}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M})^{*}\right)m_{2}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{3}\bf\right]\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{(c_{CM}^{i})^{*}}{\Lambda}\bigg(\left((f_{BM}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,A})^{*}+f_{AB}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M}\right)m_{1}\left(\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left(m_{1}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]-m_{2}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\right)-\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle^{2}\left[\bf{2}\bf\right\rvert p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{2}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+\left((f_{MA}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,B})^{*}+f_{AB}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M}\right)m_{2}\left(\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left(m_{2}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]-m_{1}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\right)+\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle^{2}\left[\bf{1}\bf\right\rvert p_{2}\left\lvert\bf{1}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+\left(f_{BM}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,A}-f_{AB}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M}\right)m_{1}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left[\bf{1}\bf\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{3}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+\left(f_{MA}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,B}-f_{AB}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M}\right)m_{2}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left[\bf{2}\bf\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{3}\right\rangle\bigg)\Bigg).

(193)

Contributions from insertions of the
F^{3}
interactions are stated in Appendix
D.4
and are less interesting.

There are three classes of helicity configurations for which the amplitude has
\sim E^{2}
HEL divergences. The first class has one longitudinal and two opposite transverse helicities. The terms in this class are given by the last two lines of (
4.3.2
) and their parity conjugates. In these cases, there are contributions from two channels, but both still contain their corresponding Mandelstam poles and remain linearly independent. These are the expected massless amplitudes generated from double insertions of
\varphi F^{2}
interactions, one of which is the emergent coupling contained in (
4.1
). These divergences can only be removed by eliminating at least one of these types of couplings.

The second class of divergent terms have two same-sign transverse polarisations and one longitudinal polarisation. In this case, the amplitude converges to contact terms in the HEL that can interfere across channels. Both (
4.3.2
) and (
4.3.2
) contribute terms. Taking the
(++,++,L)
configuration, the amplitude converges to contact terms of the form
\left[12\bf\right]^{2}
. Demanding that this cancels gives the constraint

\displaystyle(t_{C})_{im}\frac{c^{m}_{AB}}{\Lambda}-\frac{im_{3}}{2}\lambda^{i}_{CM}\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2}m_{s_{M}}}\left(f_{MA}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,B}+f_{MB}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,A}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{i}{2}\left(f_{CM}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,A}+(f_{AC}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M})^{*}\right)\frac{c^{i}_{MB}}{\Lambda}-\frac{i}{2}\left(f_{MC}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,B}+(f_{CB}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M})^{*}\right)\frac{c^{i}_{MA}}{\Lambda}=0.

(194)

If the structure constants
f_{CM}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,A}
and
f_{MC}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,B}
in the second line of (
4.3.2
) were replaced by
f_{AC}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M}
and
f_{CB}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M}
respectively, then this equation would be the covariance of the
c
-type couplings under the Lie algebra. The second term extends the internal scalar index on the
\varphi F^{2}
coupling
c^{i}_{AB}
to include the emergent scalars identified with the longitudinal vectors indexed by
M
, which is given by (
4.1
), and where the generator acting on the tensor is (
108
). Where this term exists, it is required to covariantise the couplings
c^{i}_{AB}
when
i
is a Higgs boson. Full covariance is then established by pairing this relation with (
4.2.2
), in which the external scalar index is identified with a longitudinal mode of a vector.

Double insertions of
c
-type couplings, explicit or emergent, are responsible for contaminating the interpretation of (
4.3.2
) as coupling covariance. As explained above for the counterpart relation (
4.2.2
), covariance is recovered in the limit that
c/\Lambda\ll\{f,t\}/m
(that is, the
\varphi F^{2}
are small deformations of theories with softer high energy dependence, in particular spontaneously broken YM). If the
c
-type couplings are permitted to exist, then the inclusion of contact terms
\sim E^{2}/\Lambda^{2}
in the four particle amplitude is consistent with accepted order of truncation in an EFT expansion. Violation of (
4.3.2
) by amounts
\sim m/\Lambda^{2}
would seem to be consistent with the existence of these couplings in the first place.

When one of the vectors is massless, the relation (
4.3.2
) necessarily follows from consistent factorisation through a similar argument to that sketched out around (
4.2.1
) in Section
4.2.1
above. Taking
C
as the massless gluon, then
f_{CM}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,A}=f_{AC}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M}
and
f_{MC}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,B}=f_{CB}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M}
because of (
113
), while the Higgs coupling is forbidden. The covariance constraint is therefore exact and unavoidable, although this never applies to the case in which the scalars involved are Higgs bosons.

The next step is to explicitly cancel the divergences between factorisation channels ensured by (
4.3.2
). These divergent terms can be arranged into the form of (
4.2.1
):

\displaystyle\frac{-\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]^{2}}{3m_{3}}\bigg(\left(C_{s}-C_{t}\right)\left(\frac{\left[\bf{3}\bf\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{3}\right\rangle}{s-m_{s}^{2}}-\frac{\left[\bf{3}\bf\right\rvert p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{3}\right\rangle}{t-m_{t}^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+\left(C_{t}-C_{u}\right)\left(\frac{\left[\bf{3}\bf\right\rvert p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{3}\right\rangle}{t-m_{t}^{2}}-\frac{\left[\bf{3}\bf\right\rvert p_{2}\left\lvert\bf{3}\right\rangle}{u-m_{u}^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\left(C_{u}-C_{s}\right)\left(\frac{\left[\bf{3}\bf\right\rvert p_{2}\left\lvert\bf{3}\right\rangle}{u-m_{u}^{2}}-\frac{\left[\bf{3}\bf\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{3}\right\rangle}{s-m_{s}^{2}}\right)\bigg),

(195)

where
C_{s}
,
C_{t}
and
C_{u}
are the accompanying collection of coupling constants arising from each channel in (
4.3.2
). The kinematic parts of this expression can be reduced using the syzygy (
4.2
) and (
A
) to give

\displaystyle\frac{-\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]^{2}}{m_{3}}\left(\frac{\left[\bf{3}\bf\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{3}\right\rangle}{s-m_{s}^{2}}-\frac{\left[\bf{3}\bf\right\rvert p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{3}\right\rangle}{t-m_{t}^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{-\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]}{(s-m_{s}^{2})(t-m_{t}^{2})}\big(\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{3}\right\rvert p_{1}p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{3}\right\rangle-\left(s-m_{s}^{2}\right)\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]-\left(m_{s}^{2}-m_{3}^{2}\right)\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-m_{1}\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left[\bf{3}\bf\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{1}\right\rangle-m_{2}\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{3}\bf\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\right\rangle-m_{3}\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{2}\bf\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{3}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+\frac{1}{m_{3}}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left(\left(m_{1}^{2}+m_{3}^{2}-m_{t}^{2}\right)\left[\bf{3}\bf\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{3}\right\rangle-\left(m_{3}^{2}+m_{4}^{2}-m_{s}^{2}\right)\left[\bf{3}\bf\right\rvert p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{3}\right\rangle\right)\big)

(196)

for the
s
and
t
-channel combinations, while for the
t
and
u
-channel combination,

\displaystyle\frac{-\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]^{2}}{m_{3}}\left(\frac{\left[\bf{3}\bf\right\rvert p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{3}\right\rangle}{t-m_{t}^{2}}-\frac{\left[\bf{3}\bf\right\rvert p_{2}\left\lvert\bf{3}\right\rangle}{u-m_{u}^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{-\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]}{(u-m_{u}^{2})(t-m_{t}^{2})}\big(-\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{3}\right\rvert p_{1}p_{2}\left\lvert\bf{3}\right\rangle+\left(s-m_{s}^{2}\right)\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]+m_{s}^{2}\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+m_{1}\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left[\bf{3}\bf\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{1}\right\rangle+m_{2}\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{3}\bf\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-m_{3}\left(m_{1}\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]+m_{2}\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad-\frac{1}{m_{3}}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left(\left(m_{1}^{2}+m_{3}^{2}-m_{t}^{2}\right)\left[\bf{3}\bf\right\rvert p_{2}\left\lvert\bf{3}\right\rangle-\left(m_{3}^{2}+m_{2}^{2}-m_{u}^{2}\right)\left[\bf{3}\bf\right\rvert p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{3}\right\rangle\right)\big).

(197)

The
s
and
u
combination can be obtained by applying particle exchanges to the
s
and
t
expression. Parity conjugate terms must also be added to complete this set of terms. Altogether, the expressions still have
\sim E
divergences for the helicity configurations
(++,++,--)
and
(++,++,++)
. The latter divergences occur for the single pole terms in (
4.3.2
) and (
4.3.2
). However, these terms directly cancel against those in (
4.3.2
), as expected from the fact that the massless amplitude
A(g_{A}^{+},g_{B}^{+},g_{C}^{+},\varphi_{i})
, upon which this structure should match onto in the HEL, is
0
. The
(++,++,--)
terms in (
4.3.2
), (
4.3.2
) and (
4.3.2
) directly match onto the expected massless amplitudes involving single
c
-type coupling insertions.

The remaining
\sim E^{2}
HE divergence arises in both scalar (
191
) and vector (
4.3.2
) exchange expressions above for purely longitudinal vector helicities. Contact terms have HEL scaling
\mathcal{O}(E^{3})
, so cannot contribute to further cancellation. All three factorisation channels contribute to the
\sim E^{2}
HEL with longitudinally polarised vectors. In this limit, the amplitude converges to a linear polynomial in Mandelstam variables. Requiring that this vanishes imposes the condition

\displaystyle-m_{2}\lambda^{m}_{AB}(t_{C})_{mi}+m_{3}\lambda^{i}_{CM}\left(\frac{-i}{2m_{1}m_{s_{M}}}\left(m_{s_{M}}^{2}\Re f_{AB}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M}+m_{1}^{2}\Re f_{BM}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,A}-m_{2}^{2}\Re f_{MA}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,B}\right)\right)

\displaystyle-\left(-m_{3}\lambda^{m}_{AC}(t_{B})_{mi}+m_{2}\lambda^{i}_{BM}\left(\frac{-i}{2m_{1}m_{t_{M}}}\left(m_{t_{M}}^{2}\Re f_{AC}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M}+m_{1}^{2}\Re f_{CM}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,A}-m_{3}^{2}\Re f_{MA}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,C}\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=i\Re f_{CB}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M}m_{u_{M}}\lambda^{i}_{MA}

(198)

as well as its counterparts with any of the three vector legs exchanged. This is simply the remaining “broken” index components of the Lie algebra commutator involving expressions for generator components given in (
107
) and (
108
):

\displaystyle(t_{B})_{Am}(t_{C})_{mi}+(t_{B})_{AM}(t_{C})_{Mi}-(t_{C})_{Am}(t_{B})_{mi}-(t_{C})_{AM}(t_{B})_{Mi}=i\Re f_{CB}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M}(t_{M})_{Ai}.

(199)

The couplings in (
4.3.2
) are coefficients of emergent
4
-point contact interactions between four massless scalars, with
\sim E^{2}
energy dependence. Notably, there is no such interaction that scales as
\sim E
. As a result, the condition (
4.3.2
) leads directly to full unitarisation of the longitudinal helicity configurations. However, the complete massive amplitude still contains divergent terms
\sim E
for other helicity configurations. Unsurprisingly, these correspond to single insertions of the
c
-type couplings, both those explicit in (
4.3.2
) and (
4.3.2
) and implicit in departures from (
148
).

With the relation (
199
), it has been finally established that, if the theory can be extrapolated to high energies, then the scalars, Higgs bosons and longitudinally polarised vector bosons arrange into complete representation spaces of a Lie algebra describing the vector boson self-interactions. In practice, (
4.3.2
) and (
154
) in the previous Section constrain the possible boson masses given the identity of the Lie algebra and the representation of the scalars.

It remains to reduce the three vector, one scalar amplitude to a form without terms with spurious high energy scaling. Firstly, the masses in the propagators should be homogenised as described around (
138
). Next, the terms diverging as
\sim E^{2}
in the HEL occur for fully longitudinally polarised spin configurations and converge to Mandelstam invariants, of which there are only two independent combinations. Once a basis of Lorentz structures converging to these two Mandelstam invariants has been chosen, each of the two sets of terms can be arranged to the form (
4.2.1
). For example, choosing
s
and
t
as the independent structures, then the
s
-residue terms become

\displaystyle A_{E^{2}}^{\{st\}s}(W_{A},W_{B},W_{C},\varphi_{i})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{6}\bigg(\frac{d_{s}-d_{t}}{(s-m_{s}^{2})(t-m_{t}^{2})}\mu^{2}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{3}\bf\right]+\frac{d_{u}-d_{s}}{(u-m_{u}^{2})(s-m_{s}^{2})}\mu^{2}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{3}\bf\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad-\left((u-m_{u}^{2})\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{3}\bf\right]+(t-m_{t}^{2})\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{3}\bf\right]\right)\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\left(\frac{d_{s}-d_{t}}{(s-m_{s}^{2})(t-m_{t}^{2})}+\frac{d_{t}-d_{u}}{(t-m_{t}^{2})(u-m_{u}^{2})}+\frac{d_{u}-d_{s}}{(u-m_{u}^{2})(s-m_{s}^{2})}\right)\bigg)

(200)

plus parity conjugate terms. Here,

\displaystyle d_{s}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}m_{3}}\lambda^{m}_{AB}(t_{C})_{mi}-\frac{i}{2\sqrt{2}m_{1}m_{2}m_{s_{M}}}\lambda^{i}_{CM}\left(m_{s_{M}}^{2}\Re f_{AB}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M}+m_{2}^{2}\Re f_{MA}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,B}-m_{1}^{2}\Re f_{BM}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,A}\right)

\displaystyle d_{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{i}{\sqrt{2}}\frac{m_{t_{M}}}{m_{1}m_{3}}\lambda^{i}_{BM}\Re f_{CA}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M}

\displaystyle d_{u}

\displaystyle=\frac{-1}{\sqrt{2}m_{3}}\lambda^{m}_{BC}(t_{A})_{mi}-\frac{i}{2\sqrt{2}m_{2}m_{3}m_{u_{M}}}\lambda^{i}_{AM}\left(m_{u_{M}}^{2}\Re f_{BC}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M}+m_{2}^{2}\Re f_{MA}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,B}-m_{3}^{2}\Re f_{BM}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,A}\right),

(201)

so
d_{s}+d_{t}+d_{u}=0
. The mass parameter
\mu^{2}
is as defined in (
191
). The remaining
t
-residue terms are obtained by exchanging
2\leftrightarrow 3
. The complete amplitude can be made manifestly exchange symmetric again by averaging these expressions over the distinct expressions given by particle exchanges, although I will not bother to do that here. Note that I have implicitly split-up the
s
-pole
s
-residue Lorentz structure into separate terms that converge to
t+u
in the HEL, which is the reason that (
4.3.2
) appears to contain single
s
-pole terms with
t
and
u
residues.

The divergent kinematic structure in the middle line of the right-hand side of (
4.3.2
) can be decomposed using (
A
) and (
A
). I will present the full result further below, but first focus specifically upon the single pole terms with
\sim E
scaling for two longitudinal and one transverse vector (which are the last remaining class of divergences). Collecting them all together with those remaining in (
4.3.2
) and (
4.3.2
), the
s
-channel terms are

\displaystyle A_{E}(W_{A},W_{B},W_{C},\varphi_{i})

\displaystyle=\frac{-1}{s-m_{s_{M}}^{2}}\frac{c^{i}_{MC}}{\Lambda}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\times\bigg((t_{M})_{AB}-\frac{i}{2m_{1}m_{2}}\left(m_{1}^{2}\left(({f}_{AB}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M})^{*}-({f}_{BM}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,A})^{*}\right)+m_{2}^{2}\left(({f}_{AB}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M})^{*}-({f}_{MA}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,B})^{*}\right)\right)\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{i}{2\sqrt{2}}\frac{1}{s-m_{s_{M}}^{2}}\frac{m_{s_{M}}}{m_{1}m_{2}}\lambda^{i}_{MC}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad\left(m_{1}\left({f}_{BM}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,A}-{f}_{AB}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M}\right)\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle+m_{2}\left({f}_{MA}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,B}-{f}_{AB}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M}\right)\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\right).

(202)

They match onto the expected massless amplitudes involving a single insertion of the
c
-type interaction with a vector exchange, assuming that the double insertion terms are small corrections that can be dropped. Again, these HEL divergent amplitudes are removed by eliminating the
c
-type couplings and demanding (
148
).

However, regardless of whether these single pole residuals cancel or not, the remaining amplitude, consisting of terms containing pairs of Mandelstam poles, is manifestly unitarised. No
\sim E
divergences involving
4
-point contact interactions between emerging scalar particles occur, which is consistent with the fact that no such interactions can be Lorentz invariant. Before presenting the result for the unitarised amplitude, it is first useful to establish a basis for the dimension-
4
Lorentz structures containing equal numbers of left and right-handed spinors for each external leg. Such a basis is given by the structures

\displaystyle\mathbf{T}_{s}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{3}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{3}\bf\right]

\displaystyle\mathbf{T}_{t}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\bf{31}\right\rangle\left[\bf{31}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]

\displaystyle\mathbf{T}_{u}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right],

(203)

along with the permutation (and parity) antisymmetric structure

\displaystyle{\bf{A}}_{3}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{3}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]+\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{3}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]-\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]

\displaystyle\qquad-\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{3}\bf\right]-\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{3}\bf\right]+\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right].

(204)

This is just the anomalous generalised Chern-Simons quartic (
127
) with the polarisation of particle
4
replaced with its momentum. Like the aGCS quartic, this structure vanishes in the HEL, so has weaker high-energy scaling than expected from dimensional analysis. However, unlike the aGCS quartic, it can be directly decomposed into dim-
3
Lorentz structures:

\displaystyle{\bf{A}}_{3}

\displaystyle=m_{1}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{31}\right\rangle+m_{2}\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left[\bf{31}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle+m_{3}\left\langle\bf{31}\right\rangle\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad-m_{1}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left[\bf{31}\bf\right]-m_{2}\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{31}\right\rangle\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]-m_{3}\left[\bf{31}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left[\bf{23}\bf\right].

(205)

All other non-chiral dim-
4
structures can be decomposed into this basis e.g.

\displaystyle\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{3}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]=\frac{1}{2}\left({\bf{A}}_{3}+\mathbf{T}_{s}+\mathbf{T}_{t}-\mathbf{T}_{u}\right).

(206)

Neglecting now the potential divergent single-pole terms generated by HDOs, the unitarised amplitude is then

\displaystyle A\left(W_{A},W_{B},W_{C},\varphi_{i}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{6}\Bigg(\frac{d_{s}-d_{t}}{(s-m_{s}^{2})(t-m_{t}^{2})}\mu^{2}\left(\mathbf{T}_{s}+\mathbf{T}_{t}-\mathbf{T}_{u}\right)-\frac{d_{u}-d_{s}}{(u-m_{u}^{2})(s-m_{s}^{2})}\mu^{2}\left(\mathbf{T}_{s}+\mathbf{T}_{u}-\mathbf{T}_{t}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad-\frac{d_{t}-d_{u}}{(t-m_{t}^{2})(u-m_{u}^{2})}\mu^{2}(m_{1}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left[\bf{31}\bf\right]+m_{2}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{31}\right\rangle+m_{3}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left[\bf{31}\bf\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\qquad+m_{1}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{31}\right\rangle+m_{2}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left[\bf{31}\bf\right]+m_{3}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{31}\right\rangle)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\left(\frac{d_{s}-d_{t}}{(s-m_{s}^{2})(t-m_{t}^{2})}+\frac{d_{t}-d_{u}}{(t-m_{t}^{2})(u-m_{u}^{2})}+\frac{d_{u}-d_{s}}{(u-m_{u}^{2})(s-m_{s}^{2})}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times((m_{u}^{2}-m_{2}^{2}-m_{3}^{2})\left(\mathbf{T}_{s}+\mathbf{T}_{t}-\mathbf{T}_{u}\right)-(m_{t}^{2}-m_{1}^{2}-m_{3}^{2})\left(\mathbf{T}_{s}-\mathbf{T}_{t}+\mathbf{T}_{u}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad-2m_{2}m_{3}\left(\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{3}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]+\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{3}\bf\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad-2m_{1}m_{2}\left(\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{3}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]+\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left[\bf{3}\bf\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{1}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad+2m_{3}m_{4}^{2}\left(\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{31}\right\rangle+\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left[\bf{31}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad+\left(m_{s}^{2}-m_{3}^{2}-m_{4}^{2}\right)(m_{1}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left[\bf{31}\bf\right]+m_{2}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{31}\right\rangle+m_{3}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left[\bf{31}\bf\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\,\,+m_{1}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{31}\right\rangle+m_{2}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left[\bf{31}\bf\right]+m_{3}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{31}\right\rangle))\Bigg).

(207)

As above, this is to be completed by adding terms obtained by the exchange
2\leftrightarrow 3
(I have explicitly added the parity conjugate terms in the expression above).

As expected in the HEL, the only
\sim E^{0}
terms correspond to opposite sign Compton scattering or scattering of fully longitudinal polarisations. These correctly match onto the expected massless scalar QCD amplitudes in the HEL with the emergent generators given by (
106
) and (
108
). The emergent scalar quartic contact interaction in the HEL is

\displaystyle A_{c}(W_{A}^{L},W_{B}^{L},W_{C}^{L},\varphi_{i})

\displaystyle\rightarrow\frac{-1}{4}\Bigg(\left(d_{u}+d_{u}|_{2\leftrightarrow 3}\right)\left(m_{u}^{2}-m_{2}^{2}-m_{3}^{2}\right)+\left(d_{t}-2d_{s}|_{2\leftrightarrow 3}\right)\left(m_{t}^{2}-m_{1}^{2}-m_{3}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\left(d_{t}|_{2\leftrightarrow 3}-2d_{s}\right)\left(m_{s}^{2}-m_{1}^{2}-m_{2}^{2}\right)-2\left(d_{s}+d_{s}|_{2\leftrightarrow 3}\right)\left(\sum_{i}m_{i}^{2}-2m_{4}^{2}\right)\Bigg).

(208)

While not manifest, the combination
d_{s}+d_{s}|_{2\leftrightarrow 3}
is symmetric under exchanges of the vector legs using (
4.3.2
), as are the combination of the other three terms in this expression.

Since the amplitude (
4.3.2
) is entirely proportional to Higgs couplings, its appearance in the
\mathcal{N}=4
superamplitude (
76
) is only non-trivial if the central charges of the external legs are misaligned. The resulting (colour-ordered and stripped) amplitude is then

\displaystyle A[W,W,W,\phi_{(12)}]=

\displaystyle\Big(\sin\left(\varphi_{1}+\varphi_{2}-\varphi_{3}-\varphi_{4}\right)\mathbf{T}_{s}+\sin\left(\varphi_{1}+\varphi_{3}-\varphi_{2}-\varphi_{4}\right)\mathbf{T}_{t}

\displaystyle\qquad+\sin\left(\varphi_{1}+\varphi_{3}-\varphi_{2}-\varphi_{4}\right)\mathbf{T}_{u}

\displaystyle\qquad-\sin\left(\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{4}\right)\left(\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{3}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]+\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left[\bf{3}\bf\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sin\left(\varphi_{2}-\varphi_{4}\right)\left(\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{3}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]+\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left[\bf{3}\bf\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{1}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\qquad-\sin\left(\varphi_{3}-\varphi_{4}\right)\left(\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]+\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left[\bf{1}\bf\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\qquad-m_{4}\Big(\sin\left(\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{3}\right)\left(\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle+\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\sin\left(\varphi_{3}-\varphi_{2}\right)\left(\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle+\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\sin\left(\varphi_{2}-\varphi_{1}\right)\left(\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle+\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\right)\Big)\Big)\times\frac{2i}{(s-m_{s}^{2})(u-m_{u}^{2})}.

(209)

This can be verified to agree with (
4.3.2
) once the BPS conditions on the masses are applied and the general couplings are fixed to their
\mathcal{N}=4
colour factors (including the phase dependence described in the paragraph above (
4.3.1
)). Note that, as usual, the numerator accompanying the pair of Mandelstam poles is the same for all channels in
\mathcal{N}=4
SYM.

In Appendix
D.4
, I include the contributions to this amplitude from the
F^{3}
-type couplings, which have been omitted in this analysis of this Section for clarity. They are responsible for harder HEL divergences but do not interfere with the results derived here. Table
3
below summarises the qualitative features of the amplitude from this subsection, including the Appendix.

It remains to introduce fermions into the theory of massive vector bosons and examine the constraints from tree-unitarity on their interactions. The relevant
3
-particle amplitudes involving fermions were given in Section
2.3
.

4.4.1
One vector, two fermions and one scalar

Mixed scalar-vector-fermion scattering (omitting the
u
-channel).

Each factorisation channel may be constructed as:

\displaystyle A_{t}\left(\varphi_{m},W_{A},\psi_{i},\psi_{j}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{t-m_{t_{k}}^{2}}\frac{1}{m_{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\Big((y_{ik}^{m})^{*}(t_{A})_{kj}\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert P_{t}\left\lvert\bf{3}\bf\right]-y_{ik}^{m}((t_{A})_{kj})^{*}\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{3}\right\rvert P_{t}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+y_{ik}^{m}(t_{A})_{kj}m_{t_{k}}\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]-(y_{ik}^{m})^{*}((t_{A})_{kj})^{*}m_{t_{k}}\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle\Big)

\displaystyle A_{u}\left(\varphi_{m},W_{A},\psi_{i},\psi_{j}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{u-m_{u_{k}}^{2}}\frac{1}{m_{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\Big(((t_{A})_{ik})^{*}(y_{kj}^{m})^{*}\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert P_{u}\left\lvert\bf{4}\bf\right]-(t_{A})_{ik}y_{kj}^{m}\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{4}\right\rvert P_{u}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+(t_{A})_{ik}(y_{kj}^{m})^{*}m_{u_{k}}\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]-((t_{A})_{ik})^{*}y_{jk}^{m}m_{u_{k}}\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle\Big)

\displaystyle A_{s\varphi}\left(\varphi_{m},W_{A},\psi_{i},\psi_{j}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{-1}{s-m_{s_{n}}^{2}}\frac{1}{m_{2}}(t_{A})_{mn}\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]\left((y_{ij}^{n})^{*}\left[\bf{34}\bf\right]+y_{ij}^{n}\left\langle\bf{34}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle A_{sW}\left(\varphi_{m},W_{A},\psi_{i},\psi_{j}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{-1}{s-m_{s_{M}}^{2}}\frac{\lambda_{AM}^{m}}{2\sqrt{2}m_{s_{M}}}

\displaystyle\quad\,\times\Big((t_{M})_{ij}\left(\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]\left(m_{3}\left[\bf{34}\bf\right]-m_{4}\left\langle\bf{34}\right\rangle\right)-2m_{s_{M}}^{2}\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\,\,+((t_{M})_{ij})^{*}\left(\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]\left(m_{4}\left[\bf{34}\bf\right]-m_{3}\left\langle\bf{34}\right\rangle\right)+2m_{s_{M}}^{2}\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\right)\Big).

(210)

The
s
-channel amplitude includes separate contributions from possible scalar and vector exchanges, listed respectively. Insertions of fermion dipoles and the holomorphic interactions in (
98
) are deferred to Appendix
D.5
. Both of these couplings contribute terms to the
4
-particle amplitude that scale as
\sim E
in the HEL for the transversely polarised vector, the elimination of which is only possible by eliminating the interactions themselves.

The candidate terms in the amplitude (
4.4.1
) scale as
\sim E
when the vector is longitudinally polarised and the fermions have the same helicity. All possible contact terms clearly have
\mathcal{O}(E^{2})
scaling, so cannot unitarise the amplitude and need not even be included for consistent power counting in energy. Requiring the divergence cancel imposes the following relation upon the
3
-particle coupling constants:

\displaystyle(t_{A})_{ik}y^{m}_{kj}+(t_{A})_{jk}y^{m}_{ik}+(t_{A})_{mn}y^{n}_{ij}-\frac{m_{2}}{2\sqrt{2}m_{s_{M}}}\lambda^{m}_{AM}\left(m_{4}(t_{M})_{ij}+m_{3}((t_{M})_{ij})^{*}\right)=0.

(211)

This is simply the constraint that the Yukawa couplings be a covariant tensor of the emergent Lie algebra (assuming for the moment that
(t_{A})_{ij}
are, in fact, generators, as will be shown shortly below). Since the scalars are required to combine with the longitudinal modes of the vectors into complete linear representation spaces (as shown in the previous Section), the scalar index of the Yukawa couplings must be covariantly extended to include the emergent Yukawa couplings implicit in (
20
):

\displaystyle y^{A}_{ij}=\frac{m_{j}-m_{i}}{\sqrt{2}m_{A}}\Im(t_{A})_{ij}-\frac{m_{j}+m_{i}}{\sqrt{2}m_{A}}i\Re(t_{A})_{ij}.

(212)

Then with the Higgs couplings interpreted as Lie algebra generators (
108
), (
211
) reduces to the expected statement of covariance when the free scalar index is identified with a scalar particle. Because of the form of (
212
), the covariance relation (
211
) is effectively a constraint relating the fermion masses to the Yukawa couplings, given the Lie algebra representation.

An analogous decomposition to the structure of (
4.3.1
) can be performed for the HEL divergent terms of this amplitude:

\displaystyle A_{E}\left(\varphi_{m},W_{A},\psi_{i},\psi_{j}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{-1}{2m_{2}}\left(y_{ik}^{m}(t_{A})_{jk}-y_{kj}^{m}(t_{A})_{ik}\right)\left(\frac{\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{3}\right\rvert P_{t}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]}{t-m_{t_{M}}^{2}}-\frac{\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{4}\right\rvert P_{u}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]}{u-m_{u_{M}}^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\,\quad-\frac{1}{2m_{2}}\left((t_{A})_{mn}y^{n}_{ij}-\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2}m_{s_{M}}}\lambda^{m}_{AM}\left(m_{4}(t_{M})_{ij}+m_{3}((t_{M})_{ij})^{*}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad\,\times\left(-\frac{\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{3}\right\rvert P_{t}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]}{t-m_{t_{M}}^{2}}-\frac{\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{4}\right\rvert P_{u}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]}{u-m_{u_{M}}^{2}}+\frac{2\left\langle\bf{34}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]}{s-m_{s_{M}}^{2}}\right)

(213)

and likewise for parity conjugate terms. The amplitude can be manifestly unitarised by combining divergent terms from pairs of channels. After homogenising the masses in the propagators and grouping the divergent terms in each channel into the form of (
4.2.1
), identity (
A
) can be used to combine and reduce the resulting expressions. Choosing the terms with mostly right-handed spinors and combining them in the
t
and
u
-channels, then the kinematic factor accompanying
\frac{1}{m_{2}(t-m_{t}^{2})(u-m_{u}^{2})}
from the first line becomes

\displaystyle(u-m_{u}^{2})\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{3}\right\rvert p_{1}+p_{3}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]+(t-m_{t}^{2})\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{4}\right\rvert p_{1}+p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]

\displaystyle=m_{2}m_{3}\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{3}\bf\right]+m_{2}m_{4}\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]\left[\bf{2}\bf\right\rvert p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{3}\right\rangle+m_{2}m_{4}\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{4}\bf\right]+m_{2}m_{3}\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left[\bf{2}\bf\right\rvert p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{4}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad+(u-m_{u}^{2})m_{4}\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]+(t-m_{t}^{2})m_{3}\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]-m_{2}\left\langle\bf{4}\right\rvert p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{3}\right\rvert p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{2}\left(m_{t}^{2}+m_{u}^{2}-m_{3}^{2}-m_{4}^{2}\right)\left(\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{4}\right\rvert p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]+\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{3}\right\rvert p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\left(m_{t}^{2}+m_{u}^{2}-m_{3}^{2}-m_{4}^{2}\right)\left(m_{3}\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]+m_{4}\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{2}\left(m_{t}^{2}-m_{u}^{2}+m_{3}^{2}-m_{4}^{2}\right)\left\langle\bf{34}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]-m_{1}^{2}m_{2}\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle.

(214)

The
P
-conjugate terms have complex conjugate coupling constants and a relative negative sign. Along with the remaining single pole
t
and
u
-channel terms, these represent the “Abelian” part of the amplitude. The non-Abelian parts are obtained by combining the
s
-channel terms with the
t
and
u
-channel terms in the last line of (
4.4.1
). The combination of divergent
s
and
t
terms analogous to those computed above are:

\displaystyle(t-m_{t}^{2})\left\langle\bf{34}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]+(s-m_{s}^{2})\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{3}\right\rvert p_{1}+p_{3}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]

\displaystyle=-m_{2}m_{3}\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{3}\bf\right]-m_{2}m_{4}\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]\left[\bf{2}\bf\right\rvert p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{3}\right\rangle-m_{2}m_{4}\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{4}\bf\right]-m_{2}m_{3}\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left[\bf{2}\bf\right\rvert p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{4}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad+(s-m_{s}^{2})m_{4}\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]+m_{2}\left\langle\bf{3}\right\rvert p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{4}\right\rvert p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]+m_{1}^{2}m_{2}\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad+\left(m_{s}^{2}-m_{1}^{2}-m_{2}^{2}\right)\left(m_{3}\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]+m_{3}\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{2}\left(m_{s}^{2}-m_{1}^{2}-m_{2}^{2}\right)\left(\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{3}\right\rvert p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]+\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{4}\right\rvert p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\left(m_{4}^{2}-m_{t}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\left(m_{s}^{2}-m_{1}^{2}-m_{2}^{2}\right)\right)\left\langle\bf{34}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]

(215)

(with a factor of
\frac{1}{m_{2}(s-m_{s}^{2})(t-m_{t}^{2})}
) and similarly for their
P
-conjugate terms. The
s
and
u
combined terms are similar but with
3\leftrightarrow 4
.

The complete set of remaining
\sim E^{0}
HEL terms can be checked for verification with the expected massless amplitudes. This is automatic from (
4.4.1
) for the helicity configurations in which the vector is transversely polarised, given that the relevant terms appear identically in both (
4.4.1
) and (
4.4.1
). The HEL directly matches the leading order terms onto the expected massless amplitudes (which exist for scalar, vector and same-helicity fermions), identifying the expected emergent couplings (
108
) and (
212
). It can also be checked that the longitudinally polarised vector amplitudes converges to the expected massless amplitudes (scalar scattering off opposite-helicity fermions). This limit is determined from the single pole terms remaining from the cancellation implemented in (
4.4.1
) and (
4.4.1
) in (
4.4.1
) and have to be collected together with those from (
4.4.1
). The
s
-pole terms are produced only from the
s
-channel terms in (
4.4.1
) and match onto massless gluon exchange (between scalars and fermions), while the
t
and
u
-pole terms assemble into the expected massless fermion exchange amplitudes with a Yukawa coupling given by (
212
). Finally, there are no remaining
\sim E^{0}
terms that can match onto quartic contact terms. This is expected, since there are no unitary contact terms between two scalars and two fermions.

As usual, this result can be cross-checked against the amplitude contained in (
76
) for
\mathcal{N}=4
SYM. Firstly, some relevant
3
-particle amplitudes involving fermions can be extracted from (
75
):

\displaystyle A[\lambda_{1},W,\widetilde{\lambda}^{1}]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m_{2}}\left(\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]-e^{i\left(\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{3}\right)}\left\langle\bf{32}\right\rangle\left[\bf{21}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle A[\lambda_{1},\phi_{(12)},\widetilde{\lambda}^{1}]

\displaystyle=e^{i\left(\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{2}\right)}\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]+e^{i\left(\varphi_{2}-\varphi_{3}\right)}\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle

\displaystyle A[\lambda_{1},\widetilde{\phi},\lambda_{2}]

\displaystyle=e^{i\left(\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{3}\right)}\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]+\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle.

(216)

The phases from the central charges determine the parity structure of the couplings. The amplitudes for
\lambda_{2}
are analogous but with conjugated phases. The
4
-particle amplitudes extracted from (
76
) are

\displaystyle A[\widetilde{\phi},W,\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}]

\displaystyle=\left(m_{1}\left(\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle e^{2i\varphi_{1}}-\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]e^{i(\varphi_{2}+\varphi_{3})}\right)+e^{i\varphi_{1}}\left(e^{i\varphi_{3}}\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{3}\bf\right]-e^{i\varphi_{2}}\left\langle\bf{3}\right\rvert p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]\right)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\left(m_{1}\left(\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle e^{-2i\varphi_{1}}-\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]e^{-i(\varphi_{2}+\varphi_{4})}\right)+e^{i\varphi_{1}}\left(e^{-i\varphi_{4}}\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{4}\bf\right]-e^{-i\varphi_{2}}\left\langle\bf{4}\right\rvert p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]\right)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\frac{-1}{(t-m_{t}^{2})(u-m_{u}^{2})},

(217)

which has the simple factorised form of the numerator
N_{\mathcal{N}=2}[\widetilde{\phi},\chi,\chi,\phi]\times N_{\mathcal{N}=2}[\widetilde{\phi},\chi,\phi,\chi]
(analogous to (
4.3.1
)), and

\displaystyle A[\phi_{(12)},W,\lambda_{1},\widetilde{\lambda}^{1}]

\displaystyle=\Big((u-m_{1}^{2}-m_{4}^{2})\left(e^{i(\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{2})}\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]+e^{i(\varphi_{2}+\varphi_{3}-\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{4})}\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+(t-m_{3}^{2})\left(e^{i(\varphi_{2}-\varphi_{1})}\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]+e^{i(\varphi_{1}+\varphi_{3}-\varphi_{2}-\varphi_{4})}\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+e^{i(\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{4})}\left\langle\bf{3}\right\rvert p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{4}\right\rvert p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]+e^{i(\varphi_{3}-\varphi_{1})}\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{3}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{4}\bf\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\left(im_{1}\sin\left(\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{2}\right)e^{i(\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{4})}-m_{4}e^{i\left(\varphi_{1}+\varphi_{2}-2\varphi_{4}\right)}\right)\left\langle\bf{34}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]

\displaystyle\qquad-\left(im_{1}\sin\left(\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{2}\right)e^{i(\varphi_{3}-\varphi_{1})}+m_{4}e^{i\left(\varphi_{3}+\varphi_{4}-\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{2}\right)}\right)\left[\bf{34}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]

\displaystyle\qquad-im_{1}\sin\left(\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{2}\right)\big(e^{i(\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{4})}\left(\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{4}\right\rvert p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]+\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{3}\right\rvert p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-e^{i(\varphi_{3}-\varphi_{1})}\left(\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left[\bf{4}\bf\right\rvert p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{2}\right\rangle+\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]\left[\bf{3}\bf\right\rvert p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{2}\right\rangle\right)\big)

\displaystyle\qquad-\left(m_{3}e^{i\left(\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{4}\right)}+m_{4}e^{i\left(\varphi_{3}-\varphi_{1}\right)}\right)\left(\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{3}\bf\right]+\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left[\bf{2}\bf\right\rvert p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{4}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\qquad-\left(m_{3}e^{i\left(\varphi_{3}-\varphi_{1}\right)}+m_{4}e^{i\left(\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{4}\right)}\right)\left(\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{4}\bf\right]+\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]\left[\bf{2}\bf\right\rvert p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{3}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+m_{1}\Big(\left(4im_{4}\sin\left(\varphi_{2}-\varphi_{4}\right)-m_{1}e^{-i\varphi_{2}}\right)\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+e^{i(\varphi_{3}-\varphi_{4})}\left(4im_{4}\sin\left(\varphi_{4}-\varphi_{2}\right)-m_{1}e^{i(\varphi_{2}-\varphi_{1})}\right)\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad+m_{1}^{2}\left(e^{i(\varphi_{3}-\varphi_{1})}\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]+e^{i(\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{4})}\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle\right)\Big)\times\frac{1}{(t-m_{t}^{2})(u-m_{u}^{2})}.

(218)

Applying identity (
A
) reveals that (

) also contains single pole terms and, once the BPS conditions on the masses are applied, agreement with (

) and (
