The massive fermion and vector amplitude can be constructed from
t
and
u
-channel fermion exchange:

\displaystyle A_{t}\left(W_{A},W_{B},\psi_{i},\psi_{j}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{-1}{t-m_{t_{k}}^{2}}\frac{1}{m_{1}m_{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\Big((t_{A})_{ik}(t_{B})_{kj}\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert P_{t}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]+((t_{A})_{ik})^{*}((t_{B})_{kj})^{*}\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle\left[\bf{2}\bf\right\rvert P_{t}\left\lvert\bf{1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+((t_{A})_{ik})^{*}(t_{B})_{kj}m_{t_{k}}\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle+(t_{A})_{ik}((t_{B})_{kj})^{*}m_{t_{k}}\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\Big),

(219)

and the
u
-channel exchange expression is given by swapping the vector bosons above. The
s
-channel can be mediated by a scalar or vector exchange:

\displaystyle A_{s\varphi}\left(W_{A},W_{B},\psi_{i},\psi_{j}\right)=\frac{-1}{s-m_{s_{m}}^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\lambda_{AB}^{m}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]+\frac{c^{m}_{AB}}{\Lambda}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]^{2}+\frac{(c^{m}_{AB})^{*}}{\Lambda}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\left(y_{ij}^{m}\left\langle\bf{34}\right\rangle+(y_{ij}^{m})^{*}\left[\bf{34}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle A_{sW}\left(W_{A},W_{B},\psi_{i},\psi_{j}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{-1}{s-m_{s_{M}}^{2}}\frac{i}{4m_{1}m_{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\Bigg((t_{M})_{ij}\bigg(\Re{f}_{AB}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M}(\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left(\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\left[\bf{2}\bf\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{3}\right\rangle+\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]\left[\bf{1}\bf\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{3}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left(\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{4}\bf\right]+\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{4}\bf\right]\right))

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+2\Im{f}_{AB}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M}(\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left(m_{1}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]-m_{2}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left(m_{2}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]-m_{1}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\right))

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad-\left(m_{1}{f}_{BM}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,A}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]+m_{2}({f}_{MA}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,B})^{*}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\times\left(2\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]+\frac{1}{m_{s_{M}}^{2}}\left(m_{1}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle-m_{2}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\right)\left(m_{4}\left\langle\bf{34}\right\rangle-m_{3}\left[\bf{34}\bf\right]\right)\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad-\left(m_{1}({f}_{BM}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,A})^{*}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle+m_{2}{f}_{MA}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,B}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\times\left(2\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]+\frac{1}{m_{s_{M}}^{2}}\left(m_{1}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]-m_{2}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\right)\left(m_{4}\left\langle\bf{34}\right\rangle-m_{3}\left[\bf{34}\bf\right]\right)\right)\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad\quad-((t_{M})_{ij})^{*}\bigg(\Re{f}_{AB}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M}(\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left(\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{2}\bf\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{4}\right\rangle+\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left[\bf{1}\bf\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{4}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left(\left\langle\bf{14}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{3}\bf\right]+\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{3}\bf\right]\right))

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+2\Im{f}_{AB}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M}(\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle\left(m_{1}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]-m_{2}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{14}\right\rangle\left(m_{2}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]-m_{1}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\right))

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad-\left(m_{1}{f}_{BM}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,A}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]+m_{2}({f}_{MA}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,B})^{*}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\times\left(2\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]+\frac{1}{m_{s_{M}}^{2}}\left(m_{1}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle-m_{2}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\right)\left(m_{4}\left[\bf{34}\bf\right]-m_{3}\left\langle\bf{34}\right\rangle\right)\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad-\left(m_{1}({f}_{BM}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,A})^{*}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle+m_{2}{f}_{MA}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,B}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\times\left(2\left\langle\bf{14}\right\rangle\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]+\frac{1}{m_{s_{M}}^{2}}\left(m_{1}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]-m_{2}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\right)\left(m_{4}\left[\bf{34}\bf\right]-m_{3}\left\langle\bf{34}\right\rangle\right)\right)\bigg)\Bigg).

(220)

I omit the possible contributions arising from the
h
-type
F^{3}
type couplings and the
m
-type dipole couplings in (
2.3
). For clarity of exposition, these terms are deferred to Appendix
D.6
. Their inclusion does not modify any of the analysis presented in this Section.

Now neglecting the dipole interactions, the leading order HEL divergences scale as
\sim E^{2}
for helicity configurations in which both vectors are longitudinal and the fermions are opposite. Demanding that the
\sim E^{2}
terms cancel forces the couplings to obey the Lie algebra commutator (
181
). The fermion-vector couplings are therefore identified as Lie algebra generators in some representation (thereby justifying the interpretation of (
211
) as covariance relations).

The leading HEL terms can then be combined and reduced to leave an amplitude now scaling as
\sim E
instead. Homogenising the propagator masses with the usual procedure (
138
), then just as for the scalar case, the leading divergent terms in the amplitude can be grouped into “Abelian” and “non-Abelian” components:

\displaystyle A_{E^{2}}(W_{A},W_{B},\psi_{i},\psi_{j})=

\displaystyle\qquad\frac{-1}{2m_{1}m_{2}}\left((t_{A})_{ik}(t_{B})_{kj}+(t_{B})_{ik}(t_{A})_{kj}\right)\left(\frac{\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]\left[\bf{1}\bf\right\rvert P_{t}\left\lvert\bf{2}\right\rangle}{t-m_{t}^{2}}+\frac{\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\left[\bf{2}\bf\right\rvert P_{u}\left\lvert\bf{1}\right\rangle}{u-m_{u}^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{2m_{1}m_{2}}i\Re{f}_{AB}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M}(t_{M})_{ij}\bigg(-\frac{\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]\left[\bf{1}\bf\right\rvert P_{t}\left\lvert\bf{2}\right\rangle}{t-m_{t}^{2}}+\frac{\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\left[\bf{2}\bf\right\rvert P_{u}\left\lvert\bf{1}\right\rangle}{u-m_{u}^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\frac{\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left(\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\left[\bf{2}\bf\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{3}\right\rangle+\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]\left[\bf{1}\bf\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{3}\right\rangle\right)+\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left(\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{4}\bf\right]+\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{4}\bf\right]\right)}{2(s-m_{s}^{2})}\bigg)

(221)

and likewise for the parity conjugate terms, which have been omitted for brevity, but which may be inferred by switching the shape of the spinor brackets and complex conjugating the coefficients. Using (
A
), (
A
) and preserving the manifest exchange symmetries, the Abelian part can be reduced to

\displaystyle(u-m_{u}^{2})\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]\left[\bf{1}\bf\right\rvert P_{t}\left\lvert\bf{2}\right\rangle+(t-m_{t}^{2})\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\left[\bf{2}\bf\right\rvert P_{u}\left\lvert\bf{1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\Big((u-m_{u}^{2})\left(m_{4}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle+m_{3}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad-(t-m_{t}^{2})\left(m_{4}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{14}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle+m_{3}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\Big)\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\Big(\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]\Big(m_{2}m_{3}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left[\bf{34}\bf\right]+m_{1}m_{4}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{34}\right\rangle-2m_{1}m_{2}\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\left(m_{3}^{2}+m_{4}^{2}-2m_{u}^{2}\right)\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]\Big(m_{1}m_{3}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left[\bf{34}\bf\right]+m_{2}m_{4}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{34}\right\rangle+2m_{1}m_{2}\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\left(m_{3}^{2}+m_{4}^{2}-2m_{t}^{2}\right)\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+m_{4}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left(m_{1}m_{3}\left\langle\bf{14}\right\rangle\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]-m_{2}m_{3}\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]+m_{1}m_{4}\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]-m_{2}m_{4}\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+m_{2}\left(2m_{u}^{2}-2m_{1}^{2}-m_{4}^{2}\right)\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]-m_{1}\left(2m_{t}^{2}-2m_{2}^{2}-m_{4}^{2}\right)\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]

\displaystyle\quad\quad+m_{3}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left(\left(m_{u}^{2}-m_{1}^{2}-m_{4}^{2}\right)\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]-\left(m_{t}^{2}-m_{2}^{2}-m_{4}^{2}\right)\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+m_{4}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left((m_{u}^{2}-m_{3}^{2})\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle-(m_{t}^{2}-m_{3}^{2})\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{14}\right\rangle\right)-2m_{1}m_{2}m_{4}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]^{2}\left\langle\bf{34}\right\rangle\Big).

(222)

The
s
-pole term may be separately combined with the
t
and
u
-pole terms in the non-Abelian part of (
4.4.2
):

\displaystyle-(s-m_{s}^{2})\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]\left[\bf{1}\bf\right\rvert P_{t}\left\lvert\bf{2}\right\rangle-\frac{1}{4}(t-m_{t}^{2})(\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left(\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\left[\bf{2}\bf\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{3}\right\rangle+\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]\left[\bf{1}\bf\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{3}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left(\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{4}\bf\right]+\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{4}\bf\right]\right))

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}(t-m_{t}^{2})\Big(\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]\left(m_{1}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle+m_{2}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\right)+\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\left(m_{1}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]+m_{2}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left(m_{3}\left[\bf{34}\bf\right]-m_{4}\left\langle\bf{34}\right\rangle\right)\Big)

\displaystyle\quad-(s-m_{s}^{2})m_{3}\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]\left(m_{1}m_{4}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{34}\right\rangle-m_{1}m_{2}\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle+\left(m_{s}^{2}+m_{t}^{2}-m_{1}^{2}-m_{2}^{2}-m_{3}^{2}\right)\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]\left(m_{1}m_{3}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left[\bf{34}\bf\right]+m_{1}m_{2}\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]-\left(m_{3}^{2}-m_{t}^{2}\right)\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle\quad+m_{1}(m_{2}^{2}-m_{t}^{2})\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle+m_{4}(m_{3}^{2}-m_{t}^{2})\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{14}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-m_{1}m_{3}m_{4}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle+m_{1}m_{3}m_{4}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{14}\right\rangle\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]

\displaystyle\quad+(m_{2}^{2}+m_{3}^{2}-m_{s}^{2}-m_{t}^{2})\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]\left(m_{3}\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle+m_{2}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\quad+m_{1}^{2}m_{4}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle+m_{1}m_{4}^{2}\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle-m_{1}m_{2}m_{4}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]^{2}\left\langle\bf{34}\right\rangle

(223)

for the
st
combination, while the
su
combination is obtained by exchanging
1\leftrightarrow 2
and multiplying by
(-1)
. However, the relation (
4.4.2
) applied to (
4.4.2
) in this way leads to the breaking of manifest identical fermion exchange (anti)symmetry, even among the terms with leading energy dependence. In order to restore it, I average over this expression and its image obtained by simultaneously parity conjugating and exchanging
3\leftrightarrow 4
(multiplied by
(-1)
).

Demanding full unitarisation places new constraints on the couplings. Unitarisation of the amplitudes with a single transversely polarised vector leads to the standard YM properties of the Lie algebra (
148
), at least for the subset of vectors that couple to the fermions through Lie algebra generators. The terms divergent in these limits then directly cancel once this condition is obeyed. There are also terms that scale as
\sim E
for fully transverse vectors. These are given by the
s
-channel scalar exchange in (
4.4.2
) mediated by both the
c_{AB}^{m}
and Yukawa couplings, but also appear in the vector exchange terms in (
4.4.2
) with the same Lorentz structure. These latter terms have the same form as the scalar exchange terms once the the presence of the emergent Yukawa couplings (
212
) and the emergent
c
-type couplings (
4.1
) are identified. They therefore represent the exchange of the emergent scalar particle identifiable with the longitudinal state of the vector in the HEL.

An obvious sufficient condition for the elimination of these divergences is the suppression of the
c
-type couplings, both those in (
98
) and (
4.1
) (the latter being equivalent to (
148
)). It would also seem possible that these terms could cancel their own divergences, potentially leaving behind only mass splitting terms. This would reflect a situation in which, in the UV, no scalar is actually exchanged between the vectors and the fermions through the effective
\varphi F^{2}
interaction (that is, none simultaneously participate in Yukawa couplings to the fermions and the
\varphi F^{2}
interactions, despite the presence of the latter) but where a vector exchange does still happen (this is analogous to the scenario described in Section
4.2.1
above regarding the four fermion amplitude when the mass splitting terms are introduced). Then, in order to accommodate the exchanged vector having mass in the IR, one of the UV scalars must necessarily be drawn into the process to produce the relevant terms in the vector exchange amplitude in (
4.4.2
), only for it to be canceled by a residual scalar exchange (up to mass splitting terms). In either case, unitarity requires that these terms be eliminated in such a way that they are set to zero or directly cancel.

The remaining divergences occur when the vector bosons are both longitudinally polarised and the fermions have the same helicity. Requiring that these divergences cancel imposes the constraint:

\displaystyle(t_{A})_{ik}\left(m_{4}(t_{B})_{kj}+m_{t_{k}}(t_{B})_{jk}\right)+(t_{A})_{jk}\left(m_{u_{k}}(t_{B})_{ik}+m_{3}(t_{B})_{ki}\right)-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}m_{1}m_{2}\lambda_{AB}^{m}y_{ij}^{m}

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{i}{2}\left(\Re{f}_{AB}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M}+\frac{1}{m_{s_{M}}^{2}}\left(m_{2}^{2}\Re{f}_{MA}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,B}-m_{1}^{2}\Re{f}_{BM}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,A}\right)\right)\left(m_{4}(t_{M})_{ij}+m_{3}(t_{M})_{ji}\right)=0.

(224)

Recognising (
108
) and (
107
) in the above expression identifies it as the covariance of the emergent Yukawa couplings (
212
) under the emergent Lie algebra:

\displaystyle(t_{A})_{ik}y_{kj}^{B}+(t_{A})_{jk}y_{ik}^{B}+(t_{A})_{BM}y_{ik}^{M}+(t_{A})_{Bm}y_{ik}^{m}=0,

(225)

which extends and completes the counterpart relation (
211
) to include external boson indices identified as longitudinal modes of the massive vectors. While not manifest, (
4.4.2
) is symmetric under the exchange of
1\leftrightarrow 2
. The covariance condition on the emergent couplings, such as the Yukawa couplings here, has implications for the particle spectrum of the theory since, given the Lie algebra, their existence is only possible provided that the states assemble into consistent representations.

Retaining the
c
-type couplings implicit in the three vector amplitude or, equivalently, not assuming (
148
), then these interactions can induce comparatively small terms in the amplitude that scale
\sim E/\Lambda
in the HEL if they are present but suppressed. The left hand side of (
4.4.2
) is the coupling coefficient of an emergent dim-
5

4
-particle coupling between two fermions and two scalars (accompanying the kinematic structure
\left[34\bf\right]
). This term scales as
\sim E/m
in the HEL (omitting the potentially
\sim 1
accompanying coupling constants). However, accepting the presence of other
\sim E/\Lambda
effective
3
-particle interactions, it would seem to be consistent to allow for the existence of these
4
-particle terms as well, provided that they are also of order
\sim E/\Lambda
. This will be satisfied as long as (
4.4.2
) is obeyed up to
\mathcal{O}\left(m/\Lambda\right)
corrections, although it is remarkable that, unlike the other dim-
5
effective interactions discussed in this Section (like the fermion dipole couplings and
\varphi F^{2}
), the covariance of the Yukawa couplings remains exact even when the vector self-couplings merely obey the partial unitarisation conditions but depart from standard YM.

Accepting the conditions required to eliminate all remaining HEL divergences, the remaining
\sim E
divergent terms in the amplitude can be again grouped into a
tu
“Abelian” part and a remaining “non-Abelian” part as

\displaystyle A_{E}(W_{A},W_{B},\psi_{i},\psi_{j})=

\displaystyle\qquad\frac{-\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]}{2m_{1}m_{2}}\left(m_{t_{k}}(t_{A})_{ik}(t_{B})_{jk}-m_{u_{k}}(t_{B})_{ik}(t_{A})_{jk}+\frac{i}{2}f_{ABM}\left(m_{4}(t_{M})_{ij}-m_{3}(t_{M})_{ji}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\left(\frac{\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle}{t-m_{t}^{2}}+\frac{\left\langle\bf{14}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle}{u-m_{u}^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]}{2m_{1}m_{2}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}m_{1}m_{2}\lambda_{AB}^{m}y_{ij}^{m}-\frac{i}{2}f_{ABM}\frac{m_{1}^{2}-m_{2}^{2}}{m_{s_{M}}^{2}}\left(m_{4}(t_{M})_{ij}+m_{3}(t_{M})_{ji}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\left(\frac{2\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{34}\right\rangle}{s-m_{s}^{2}}+\frac{\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle}{t-m_{t}^{2}}-\frac{\left\langle\bf{14}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle}{u-m_{u}^{2}}\right)

(226)

and similarly for parity conjugate terms given by switching the shape of spinor brackets and complex conjugating the coefficients. Obviously my use of the words “Abelian” and “non-Abelian” parts here have been made to parallel the structure of (
4.4.2
) and are not intended as precise descriptions of the coupling algebra. Pairs of terms can be combined by applying (
4.2
) to manifest the cancellation of the HEL. This results in e.g. the
t
and
u
-pole terms combining in the “Abelian” part as

\displaystyle(u-m_{u}^{2})\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle+(t-m_{t}^{2})\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{14}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\Big(\left[\bf{1}\bf\right\rvert p_{4}-p_{3}\left\lvert\bf{2}\right\rangle\left(m_{1}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{34}\right\rangle-m_{3}\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{14}\right\rangle+m_{4}\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\left[\bf{2}\bf\right\rvert p_{4}-p_{3}\left\lvert\bf{1}\right\rangle\left(m_{2}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{34}\right\rangle+m_{3}\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle-m_{4}\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\quad-2m_{3}m_{4}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left(\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]+\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\left(m_{1}^{2}+m_{2}^{2}-2m_{t}^{2}\right)\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{14}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle+\left(m_{1}^{2}+m_{2}^{2}-2m_{u}^{2}\right)\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left(m_{1}m_{4}\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle+m_{2}m_{4}\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle+m_{1}m_{3}\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle+m_{2}m_{3}\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{14}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left(m_{1}m_{4}\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle+m_{2}m_{4}\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle+m_{1}m_{3}\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{14}\right\rangle+m_{2}m_{3}\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle\right)\Big).

(227)

The kinematic parts of the other channels can be combined to give an identical expression by (
4.2
) up to some subleading terms dependent upon the different internal masses. I leave the expression in the much less symmetric form above for the purposes of reconciling it with the
\mathcal{N}=4
SYM expression presented further below, rather than preserving the simplicity of (
4.2
).

It remains to check that the unitarised amplitude matches correctly onto the expected massless amplitudes in the HEL. Firstly, it is immediately clear that the terms corresponding to both transversely polarised vectors correctly match onto the expected massless gluon Compton amplitude. These terms are given by the third terms in the third and fifth lines of (
4.4.2
) and the second terms in the third and fourth lines of the right-hand side of (
4.4.2
), when substituted into (
4.4.2
). There is no contribution from the reduced
\sim E
terms in (
4.4.2
) above. Secondly are the terms corresponding to a single transverse vector. While much less obvious, these can be compiled by collecting the relevant contributing terms in (
4.4.2
), (
4.4.2
), (
4.4.2
), (
4.4.2
), and (
4.4.2
) and their parity conjugates, while identifying the emergent scalar couplings in (
106
) and (
212
). Only a single Lorentz structure contributes to each helicity configuration, so is common to all such terms (e.g.
\left[\bf{2}\bf\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{1}\right\rangle\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{34}\right\rangle
for the configuration
(L,++,-,-)
). The accompanying combinations of coupling constants then agree with their expected expressions on the residues of each channel.

This leaves the terms with both vectors longitudinally polarised. In this case, there are two linearly independent Lorentz structures for each possible fermion chirality configuration. For example, for the
(L,L,-,+)
configuration, these are
\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]
and
\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]
, which match onto
\frac{1}{4}t\left[14\bf\right]\left\langle 13\right\rangle
and
-\frac{1}{4}u\left[14\bf\right]\left\langle 13\right\rangle
respectively in the HEL. Because these contain Mandelstam variables, each of the reduced terms in the massive amplitude match onto terms containing only a single massless pole in the HEL. Additionally, the leading order mass splitting terms also contribute. As required, these partially cancel against some of the terms that contain internal masses in (
4.4.2
) and (
4.4.2
) when substituted into (
4.4.2
), effectively replacing them with their physical values to ensure that the emergent couplings are correct. Some of the internal masses remain and cancel directly among themselves, which could be made manifest by making some different basis choices in the kinematic numerators of each pair of channels. When the cancellations are not direct, they can be made manifest by applying variations of the identities (
A
) and (
A
). However, as with the mixed amplitudes with scalars above, I will not bother to explicitly demonstrate these cancellations in the expression already provided for the full massive amplitude. I will simply note that the unitarised massive amplitude does correctly match onto the expected massless amplitudes in the HEL. The
t
and
u
-channels match onto scalar-fermion scattering amplitude mediated by the emergent scalar-fermion Yukawa couplings, while the
s
-channel matches onto scalar-fermion scattering mediated by a gluon. Finally, as for the previous mixed case, there is no emergent quartic contact coupling, as expected from the fact that there is no corresponding renormalisable operator.

As usual, the fermion-vector amplitude can be compared with the special case of
\mathcal{N}=4
SYM. The fermion-vector colour-stripped amplitude extracted from (
76
) is

\displaystyle A[W,W,\lambda_{1},\widetilde{\lambda}^{1}]

\displaystyle=\frac{-1}{(s-m_{s}^{2})(u-m_{u}^{2})}\epsilon_{\varphi}(1,2,3,4)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\left(m_{4}\left(\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle e^{-2i\varphi_{4}}-\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]e^{-i(\varphi_{1}+\varphi_{2})}\right)+e^{-i\varphi_{4}}\left(e^{-i\varphi_{2}}\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]-e^{-i\varphi_{1}}\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]\right)\right)

(228)

which has numerator with the factorised form
N_{\mathcal{N}=2}[\chi,\chi,\chi,\chi]\times N_{\mathcal{N}=2}[\chi,\chi,\phi,\widetilde{\phi}]
. This can be checked for agreement with the combined terms in (
4.4.2
), (
4.4.2
) and (
4.4.2
) once the BPS condition is applied to the masses (I admit that I only did this myself for the Abelian part).

summarises the qualitative conclusions of this analysis. Again,
