5
Conclusion

In quantum mechanical descriptions of interacting particles, the combined principles of Lorentz invariance, unitarity and the specific analytic structure of scattering amplitudes are sufficient to bootstrap much of fundamental physics. Assuming that two-to-two scattering amplitudes contain only simple poles as singularities (when viewed as a function of helicity spinors), then the residues of these poles must describe on-shell propagation of an intermediary particle. For massless particles however, these residues can themselves contain poles that must admit a consistent interpretation of a cross-channel exchange. Consistent factorisation places powerful constraints on permissible structures of the interactions. In this study, I present the complete derivation of the coupling structure of perturbative Yang-Mills from these principles (among other details), addressing some of the questions left open by
94
.

With massive particles, Lorentz invariance allows for a broader range of independent structures appearing in on-shell amplitudes and consistent factorisation is less demanding. Instead, a successive hierarchy of constraints emerges from demanding degrees of suppression of high energy growth. A partial unitarisation of the amplitudes to the standards of gravity enforces the Lie algebra structure of the
P
and
T
-symmetric parts of the massive vector boson self-couplings and also allows for
P
and
T
-violating Generalised Chern-Simons terms. Demanding full unitarisation leads to the further specifications on the Lie algebra expected for Yang-Mills theory, while the longitudinally polarised vectors are required to combine with the scalar particles into a valid representation space. Minimal matter couplings to vectors are required to be Lie algebra generators, while all others, including those involving longitudinally polarised vectors, must be (approximately) covariant tensors respecting the representations. More broadly, I examine the individual conditions required for the unitarisation of each helicity sector in the massive amplitudes and the space of EFTs that emerge from select subsets of conditions.

Finally, I calculate, from first principles, all two-to-two tree-level scattering amplitudes involving massive particles with spin
s\leq 1
and present them in a form in which their high-energy dependence in manifest. The unitarised expressions for the amplitudes are reconciled with the especially elegant results for
\mathcal{N}=2
super-QED and
\mathcal{N}=4
super-Yang-Mills, which are entirely determined by supersymmetry and the BPS condition.

In a companion work
109
, I extend these results to include contributions from graviton exchanges and also investigate connections with supersymmetric theories. Consistent factorisation is used to complete the first-principles derivation of supergravity from the presence of a massless helicity-
3/2
state. It is also shown that supersymmetric theories of massive BPS particles, with or without gravity, are further constrained by consistent factorisation of superamplitudes, which mandates the emergence of further order on the permissible couplings. In particular, with
\mathcal{N}=2
supersymmetry, the Jacobi and GCS conditions are forced by self-consistency, affirming the above picture emerging from the weaker requirement of suppressed high-energy scaling. I also elaborate more on the details of the elegant expressions for the
4
-particle amplitudes of BPS particles in extended SUSY theories and identify the special properties of the two equal mass, one massless
3
-particle amplitudes as a special case of general BPS kinematics.

However, other avenues of further work remain. The crossing relations between amplitudes used here and in
109
are established either by direct comparison with the Feynman rules or by intelligent guessing. Missing is a first principles derivation (in QFT or otherwise). To my knowledge, this was never done adequately for spinning particles. In any case, the details of the analytic continuation of the (spinning) amplitudes were able to be sidestepped to obtain the results presented here, but an overall sign was still required.

The bootstrap methodology deduces necessity but not sufficiency. The conditions on the couplings and masses derived here are necessary for suppressed high-energy dependence of the general
4
-leg massive amplitudes that were explicitly calculated. These conditions agree with the expected and known results established in field theory. The existence of examples validates the conclusions. Extending the analysis here to include loops is an obvious further direction. It has been assumed here that loops are suppressed, but a power counting rule justifying this and guiding a hierarchical ordering of terms in a loop expansion remains a vital further ingredient for a consistent EFT (see
78
for examples). This should exist for a gauged non-linear sigma model, but is unclear for a theory of less structured trivalent couplings. Perturbative unitarity may continue to provide a guide toward this. Establishing the precise relation between the space of consistent EFTs and the space of possible Lorentz structures is an interesting question. The question of convergence is closely related, see egs
5
;
4
for recent commentary in the context of HEFT. The off-shell picture of potentials and field geometry is also something that warrants further investigation
44
;
62
;
5
;
70
. In this study, I have identified the relation between the emergent contact interactions and masses and couplings, but not reconstructed the vacuum structure, simply asserting agreement with the expected theories that the HEL matches onto.

Implicit in the “high-energy limit” has been the assumption of a mass gap involving the intermediate exchanged states for each tested amplitude. The emergent Lie algebra representations require the existence of a Higgs boson for a gapped theory to be unitary. Examples of Higgsless Kaluza-Klein Yang-Mills theories exist
48
;
57
in which each amplitude between mass eigenstates has unitary UV behaviour by the standards of this analysis. These theories have properties consistent with the results of this study in spite of not being gapped and not being unitary at high energies. However, the role of the mass gap and the consequences of an unbounded, infinite tower of tree-level exchanges remains to be explored with the level of generality maintained here. See e.g.
25
for some discussion of this issue for Kaluza-Klein theories. Exchanges of massive higher spin states also have not been considered here. Obviously this has been a subject of much study in the past
111
;
118
that continue today (see eg.
15
;
2
;
21
for a handful of recent studies - obviously this subject is vast).

The massive
4
-particle amplitudes calculated in this study were determined entirely from first principles through unitarity methods. They were inferred from demanding that they reproduce the required factorisation channels, which are simply calculated by combining two
3
-particle amplitudes together for kinematic configurations with intermediate on-shell states. Much of the subsequent effort was required to then convert the expression into a form in which the high-energy dependence was manifest. Calculating higher leg amplitudes in this way would be much more complicated. On-shell recursion relations have been successfully used to efficiently calculate multi-leg tree amplitudes for numerous massless theories
30
;
29
;
34
;
103
. Developing these for massive theories remains an open issue. Some recent advances have been made in
74
;
73
;
80
which adapted the all-line shift of
53
to massive theories. These studies demonstrated close connection between the terms in the expansion and a Feynman rule representation for the amplitude under an intelligent gauge choice. The imprints of the Ward identities were also demonstrated as underpinning theories with suppressed high-energy dependence that were amenable to the technique. Alternatively, BCFW recursion
30
is valid for spontaneously broken
\mathcal{N}=4
SYM
86
(since it can be embedded in massless YM in higher dimensions, where BCFW is known to be valid
10
;
8
;
67
;
27
). For massless theories, BCFW is much more efficient than the all-line shift and, for
\mathcal{N}=4
broken SYM, leads directly to the manifestly high energy unitary representation (
76
) containing (
173
). However, this method (or any other related idea egs.
33
;
3
) has never been used to calculate massive superamplitudes beyond five legs (where the SUSY structure begins to become non-trivial) in an insightful way, although it should be possible in theory. The significance and usefulness of the different representations of the massive amplitudes (with or without “spurious” high energy dependence and with or without residues with other “spurious” poles) and the implications for on-shell methods remains open for exploration.

Performing an analogous analysis for other spacetime backgrounds (AdS, dS, inflation, FRW) is another obvious direction
16
.

Appendix A
Horrible Products of Bilinears

Here is a list of useful algebraic relations and “syzygies” for
4
-particle on-shell amplitudes. These relate Lorentz structures of a particular mass dimension to combinations of Lorentz structures of smaller mass dimension (“syzygies” being such relations that only exist when certain combinations of Mandelstam variables are multiplying the otherwise linearly independent spinor structures). They are useful for manifesting cancellations between combinations of contact and factorisation terms, but may possibly find use in the on-shell operator basis construction of EFTs
58
;
66
;
69
;
68
.

The identity

\displaystyle(4t-\sum_{i}m_{i}^{2})\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\left[\bf{32}\bf\right]+(4u-\sum_{i}m_{i}^{2})\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{42}\bf\right]

\displaystyle\qquad=m_{1}\left(\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{2}-p_{3}\left\lvert\bf{4}\bf\right]+\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{2}-p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{3}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+m_{2}\left(\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{1}-p_{3}\left\lvert\bf{4}\bf\right]+\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{1}-p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{3}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+m_{3}\left(\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{3}\right\rvert p_{1}-p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]+\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{3}\right\rvert p_{2}-p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+m_{4}\left(\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{4}\right\rvert p_{2}-p_{3}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]+\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{4}\right\rvert p_{1}-p_{3}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad-2\left(m_{1}m_{3}\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]+m_{1}m_{4}\left\langle\bf{14}\right\rangle\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]+m_{2}m_{3}\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]+m_{2}m_{4}\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\right)

was found in
68
(in a much less manifestly symmetric form). In the
\{\mathbf{L}_{i}\}
basis (
4.2
), (
A
) becomes

\displaystyle(t-\frac{1}{3}\sum_{i}m_{i}^{2})\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\left[\bf{32}\bf\right]+(u-\frac{1}{3}\sum_{i}m_{i}^{2})\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{42}\bf\right]=\frac{1}{12}\sum_{i}\mathbf{L}_{i}.

(230)

This relation encapsulates the deformation of the massless all-channel pole (
3
) to massive kinematics, as explained in Section
4.2
.

The following identities have application to the four massive vector amplitude:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m_{1}^{2}}\mathbf{L}_{1}\hat{\mathbf{L}}_{1}

\displaystyle\quad=24\big((s-m_{s}^{2})\left(\mathbf{P}_{t}+\mathbf{P}_{u}-\mathbf{P}_{s}\right)+(t-m_{t}^{2})\left(\mathbf{P}_{u}+\mathbf{P}_{s}-\mathbf{P}_{t}\right)+(u-m_{u}^{2})\left(\mathbf{P}_{s}+\mathbf{P}_{t}-\mathbf{P}_{u}\right)\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+2\Big(\left(\left\langle\bf{14}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle+\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle\right)\mathbf{L}_{2}+\left(\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]+\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]\right)\hat{\mathbf{L}}_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\left(\left\langle\bf{14}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle-\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{34}\right\rangle\right)\mathbf{L}_{3}-\left(\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]-\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left[\bf{34}\bf\right]\right)\hat{\mathbf{L}}_{3}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\left(\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{34}\right\rangle+\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle\right)\mathbf{L}_{4}-\left(\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left[\bf{34}\bf\right]+\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]\right)\hat{\mathbf{L}}_{4}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad-36\Big(m_{1}m_{2}\left[\bf{34}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{34}\right\rangle\left(\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]^{2}+\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle^{2}\right)+m_{3}m_{4}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left(\left[\bf{34}\bf\right]^{2}+\left\langle\bf{34}\right\rangle^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad+m_{1}m_{3}\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle\left(\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]^{2}+\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle^{2}\right)+m_{2}m_{4}\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left(\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]^{2}+\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad+m_{2}m_{3}\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{14}\right\rangle\left(\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]^{2}+\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle^{2}\right)+m_{1}m_{4}\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left(\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]^{2}+\left\langle\bf{14}\right\rangle^{2}\right)\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad-36\Big(m_{3}m_{4}\left(\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{14}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle+\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad+m_{2}m_{3}\left(\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{34}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle+\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle\left[\bf{34}\bf\right]\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad-m_{2}m_{4}\left(\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{34}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{14}\right\rangle+\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left[\bf{34}\bf\right]\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\right)\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad+4\left(m_{2}^{2}\left(\mathbf{P}_{s}+4\left(\mathbf{P}_{t}+\mathbf{P}_{u}\right)\right)+m_{3}^{2}\left(\mathbf{P}_{t}+4\left(\mathbf{P}_{s}+\mathbf{P}_{u}\right)\right)+m_{4}^{2}\left(\mathbf{P}_{u}+4\left(\mathbf{P}_{s}+\mathbf{P}_{t}\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+24\left(m_{s}^{2}\left(\mathbf{P}_{t}+\mathbf{P}_{u}-\mathbf{P}_{s}\right)+m_{t}^{2}\left(\mathbf{P}_{u}+\mathbf{P}_{s}-\mathbf{P}_{t}\right)+m_{u}^{2}\left(\mathbf{P}_{s}+\mathbf{P}_{t}-\mathbf{P}_{u}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+8\left(\mathbf{P}_{s}+\mathbf{P}_{t}+\mathbf{P}_{u}\right)

(231)

and

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m_{1}m_{2}}\mathbf{L}_{2}\hat{\mathbf{L}}_{1}

\displaystyle\quad=144\left(s-m_{s}^{2}\right)\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad-2\frac{m_{2}}{m_{1}}\left(\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]+\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\right)\hat{\mathbf{L}}_{1}-2\frac{m_{1}}{m_{2}}\left(\left\langle\bf{14}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle+\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\right)\mathbf{L}_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad-36m_{3}m_{4}\left(\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle^{2}\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]^{2}+\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle^{2}\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+36\left[\bf{34}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{34}\right\rangle\left(m_{1}^{2}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle^{2}+m_{2}^{2}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+3m_{1}m_{4}\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle\left(6\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle-14\left(\left\langle\bf{14}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle-\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{34}\right\rangle\right)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+3m_{1}m_{3}\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left(6\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{14}\right\rangle-14\left(\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle+\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{34}\right\rangle\right)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+3m_{2}m_{4}\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left(6\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]-14\left(\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]+\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left[\bf{34}\bf\right]\right)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+3m_{2}m_{3}\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle\left(6\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]-14\left(\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]-\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left[\bf{34}\bf\right]\right)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+72\Big(m_{2}m_{3}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{34}\right\rangle-m_{2}m_{4}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{34}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad+m_{1}m_{4}\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\left[\bf{34}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle-m_{1}m_{3}\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{34}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{2}\left(4m_{s}^{2}-m_{3}^{2}-m_{4}^{2}\right)\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad-4m_{1}m_{2}\left(17\mathbf{P}_{s}+2\left(\mathbf{P}_{t}+\mathbf{P}_{u}\right)\right).

(232)

These can be combined together to give

\displaystyle\sum_{j}\mathbf{L}_{j}\sum_{k}\hat{\mathbf{L}}_{k}

\displaystyle\quad=144m_{1}m_{2}(s-m_{s}^{2})\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle+\text{ex.}

\displaystyle\qquad+24\sum_{i}m_{i}^{2}\big((s-m_{s}^{2})\left(\mathbf{P}_{t}+\mathbf{P}_{u}-\mathbf{P}_{s}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+(t-m_{t}^{2})\left(\mathbf{P}_{u}+\mathbf{P}_{s}-\mathbf{P}_{t}\right)+(u-m_{u}^{2})\left(\mathbf{P}_{s}+\mathbf{P}_{t}-\mathbf{P}_{u}\right)\big)

\displaystyle\qquad-144\left(\prod_{i}m_{i}\right)\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle^{2}\left[\bf{34}\bf\right]^{2}+\text{ex.}

\displaystyle\qquad+144m_{1}m_{2}\left(m_{s}^{2}-m_{3}^{2}-m_{4}^{2}\right)\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle+\text{ex.}

\displaystyle\qquad-16(m_{1}m_{2})^{2}\left(8\mathbf{P}_{s}-\left(\mathbf{P}_{t}+\mathbf{P}_{u}\right)\right)+\text{ex.}

\displaystyle\qquad+24\sum_{i}m_{i}^{2}\left(m_{s}^{2}\left(\mathbf{P}_{t}+\mathbf{P}_{u}-\mathbf{P}_{s}\right)+m_{t}^{2}\left(\mathbf{P}_{u}+\mathbf{P}_{s}-\mathbf{P}_{t}\right)+m_{u}^{2}\left(\mathbf{P}_{s}+\mathbf{P}_{t}-\mathbf{P}_{u}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+8\sum_{i}m_{i}^{4}\left(\mathbf{P}_{s}+\mathbf{P}_{t}+\mathbf{P}_{u}\right),

(233)

where “ex.” indicates all distinct terms obtained by exchanging particle labels in the term listed in the same line.

Other useful identities are

\displaystyle\frac{1}{6}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\frac{1}{m_{3}}\mathbf{L}_{3}-\frac{1}{6}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\frac{1}{m_{4}}\hat{\mathbf{L}}_{4}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3}m_{4}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left(\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle+\left\langle\bf{14}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\right)-\frac{1}{3}m_{3}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left(\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]+\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\left(m_{1}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle-m_{2}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\right)\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]+\left(m_{2}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle-m_{1}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\right)\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]

(234)

and

\displaystyle\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{3}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{3}\right\rvert p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{4}\bf\right]-\left(u-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i}m_{i}^{2}\right)\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad=-\frac{1}{2}\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left(m_{4}\left\langle\bf{4}\right\rvert p_{3}-p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]+m_{2}\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{3}-p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{4}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-m_{1}m_{3}\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]+\frac{1}{2}\left(m_{1}^{2}+m_{3}^{2}\right)\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle

(235)

and

\displaystyle\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]

\displaystyle\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{3}\bf\right]+\left(m_{1}^{2}+m_{4}^{2}-u\right)\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad=m_{4}^{2}\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle-m_{1}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{3}\bf\right]-m_{1}\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{1}\bf\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+m_{3}\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]-m_{2}\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]

(236)

and

\displaystyle\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{3}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]-\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{3}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\left(t-u\right)\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad-\frac{1}{2}\left(m_{1}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle+m_{2}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\right)\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{3}-p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]-\frac{1}{2}\left(m_{2}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle+m_{1}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\right)\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{3}-p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]

(237)

and

\displaystyle\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left[\bf{3}\bf\right\rvert p_{1}p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{3}\bf\right]+\left(s-m_{1}^{2}-m_{2}^{2}\right)\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]

\displaystyle\qquad=\left(m_{1}^{2}-m_{2}^{2}+m_{3}^{2}\right)\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]-m_{1}\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{3}\bf\right]-m_{3}\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{3}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]-m_{2}\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{3}\bf\right]

(238)

and

\displaystyle\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{3}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]-(u-m_{1}^{2}-m_{4}^{2})\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=m_{4}^{2}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle-\left(m_{2}\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle-m_{3}\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\right)\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]-\left(m_{3}\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle-m_{2}\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\right)\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{3}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{2}m_{1}\left(\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left(\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{3}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]+\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]\right)+\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left(\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{3}\bf\right]+\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]\right)\right).

(239)

and the syzygies

\displaystyle\left(t-m_{1}^{2}-m_{3}^{2}\right)\left\langle\bf{3}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{3}\bf\right]-\left(s-m_{3}^{2}-m_{4}^{2}\right)\left\langle\bf{3}\right\rvert p_{1}\left\lvert\bf{3}\bf\right]=m_{3}\left(\left[\bf{3}\bf\right\rvert p_{1}p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{3}\bf\right]+\left\langle\bf{3}\right\rvert p_{1}p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{3}\right\rangle\right)

(240)

(which was mentioned already in (
4.2
)) and

\displaystyle-(u-m_{1}^{2}-m_{4}^{2})\left\langle\bf{21}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{3}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]-(s-m_{3}^{2}-m_{4}^{2})\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]

\displaystyle=-(t-m_{2}^{2}-m_{4}^{2})\left(m_{3}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]+m_{1}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\right)+m_{2}\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{3}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+m_{2}\left(m_{3}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{3}\bf\right]-m_{1}\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]+m_{1}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{3}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]-m_{3}\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+m_{4}^{2}\left(\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]-\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{3}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]\right)+\left(m_{3}^{2}-m_{1}^{2}\right)\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]-m_{2}m_{4}^{2}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle

(241)

and

\displaystyle\left(s-m_{1}^{2}-m_{2}^{2}\right)\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]+\left(t-m_{2}^{2}-m_{4}^{2}\right)\left(u-m_{1}^{2}-m_{4}^{2}\right)\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad=m_{1}\left(t-m_{2}^{2}-m_{4}^{2}\right)\left(\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]+\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-m_{2}\left(u-m_{1}^{2}-m_{4}^{2}\right)\left(\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]+\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+m_{1}m_{2}\left(\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]^{2}+\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]^{2}\right)+m_{4}^{2}\left(s-m_{1}^{2}-m_{2}^{2}\right)\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+m_{1}m_{2}m_{4}^{2}\left(\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle^{2}+\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]^{2}\right).

(242)

Reversing the spinor chiralities gives analogous identities. The latter two syzygies can both be derived by contracting various bilinears against (
230
).

Appendix B
Special 3-Particle Massless Kinematics

Here I just give some simple complex momentum configurations that realise the on-shell factorisation channels for the four leg amplitudes
A(1,2\rightarrow 3,4)
of massless particles.

Denote by
p_{i}
the real-valued external particle momenta chosen so that the amplitude factorises on the
s
-channel. Define
\hat{p}_{i}
as the corresponding on-shell momenta under a complex deformation made so that on the amplitude remains factorised on the
s
-channel. Then within the
3
-particle factor amplitude
A(\hat{1},\hat{2},\hat{P}_{s})
, the momenta are

\displaystyle\hat{p}_{1}

\displaystyle=p_{1}=E_{1}(1,0,0,1)

\displaystyle\hat{p}_{2}

\displaystyle=p_{2}-r,\qquad p_{2}=E_{2}(1,0,0,1)

\displaystyle P_{s}

\displaystyle=-(p_{1}+p_{2})

\displaystyle\hat{P}_{s}

\displaystyle=-(\hat{p}_{1}+\hat{p}_{2})=-(p_{1}+p_{2})+r=P_{s}+r,

(243)

where
E_{1,2}
are the particles’ energies and
r
is a complex null vector satisfying
r\cdot r=0
and
r\cdot p_{2}=0
. A possible choice is
r=z(0,1,\pm i,0)
for some constant
z
. The corresponding spinors are then

\displaystyle\left\lvert i\bf\right]=\begin{pmatrix}0\\
\sqrt{2E_{i}}\end{pmatrix}\qquad\qquad\left\langle i\right\rvert=\begin{pmatrix}0&\sqrt{2E_{i}}\end{pmatrix}

(244)

for either
i=1,2
. The choice of sign in
r
corresponds to choice of spinors

\displaystyle\left\lvert r_{+}\bf\right]=\begin{pmatrix}-z\sqrt{2/e}\\
0\end{pmatrix}\qquad\qquad\left\langle r_{+}\right\rvert=\begin{pmatrix}0&\sqrt{2e}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\left\lvert r_{-}\bf\right]=\begin{pmatrix}0\\
\sqrt{2e}\end{pmatrix}\qquad\qquad\left\langle r_{-}\right\rvert=\begin{pmatrix}z\sqrt{2/e}&0\end{pmatrix}.

(245)

where the factor of
e
represents the
GL(1,\mathbb{C})
rescaling redundancy in the complexified spinors. The choice of
\pm
clearly corresponds to the choice of alignment of each chirality of spinor.

On the opposite side of the channel, the outgoing particles can be treated similarly. The complex momentum deformation can be absorbed into a shift of leg
4
in exactly the same way that it deforms
p_{2}
. This describes the limit
\left\langle\hat{1}\hat{2}\right\rangle,\left\langle\hat{3}\hat{4}\right\rangle\rightarrow 0
or
\left[\hat{1}\hat{2}\bf\right],\left[\hat{3}\hat{4}\bf\right]\rightarrow 0
, which, of course, activates all
s,t,u
-channels simultaneously and describes the all-channel pole. The channels with vanishing spinor bilinears of opposite chirality on each side requires
p_{2}
and
p_{4}
to be instead deformed by
r
and
r^{*}
respectively, rather than both by
r
, as is enforced by momentum conservation above. This alternative can be arranged by also deforming
p_{1}
and
p_{3}
by
-r
and
-r^{*}
respectively as well, which ensures that either
\left\langle\hat{1}\hat{2}\right\rangle,\left[\hat{3}\hat{4}\bf\right]\rightarrow 0
or
\left[\hat{1}\hat{2}\bf\right],\left\langle\hat{3}\hat{4}\right\rangle\rightarrow 0
.

Appendix C
Multipoles and the Geometry of Gauge Theories

This Appendix links vector boson cubic couplings to Lie algebra geometry for a simple theory consisting of a massless photon and a charge-conjugate pair of
W
-bosons.The massless photon immediately demands conserved electric charge. There are therefore two possible
T
-symmetric couplings parameterising a Lie algebra and one independent
T
-violating GCS coupling. The Lie algebra describing the
T
-symmetric couplings is, in general, defined by the commutation relations

\displaystyle[T_{W_{1}},T_{W_{2}}]=is\alpha T_{\gamma}\qquad\qquad[T_{\gamma},T_{W_{1}}]=i\beta T_{W_{2}}\qquad\qquad[T_{\gamma},T_{W_{2}}]=-i\beta T_{W_{1}}.

(246)

Here
T_{\gamma}
is the generator associated with the photon and
T_{W_{1}}
and
T_{W_{2}}
are the generators of the
W
-bosons in a self-conjugate representation. The signature of the Killing form is determined by
s\in\{0,\pm 1\}
. The parameters
\alpha,\beta>0
are continuous and describe the normalisations of the photon and
W
-boson generators. On-shell,
\beta
corresponds to the gauge coupling, while the ratio
s\alpha/\beta
parameterises the magnetic dipole moments of the
W
-bosons (as well as the electric quadrupole moment). The
3
-particle amplitude is then

\displaystyle A\left(W_{1},W_{2},\gamma^{+}\right)

\displaystyle=i\beta\left(\frac{1}{mx}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle^{2}-\frac{1-s\alpha/\beta}{2m^{2}}\left[3\bf{2}\bf\right]\left[3\bf{1}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\right).

(247)

The electric dipole moment (and magnetic quadrupole moment) corresponds to the possible GCS term, which may be accounted for by complexifying
\alpha
in this expression.

In general, the Lie algebra is identified as
\mathfrak{su}_{2}
if
s=1
and, furthermore, the case of homogeneous generator normalisation required for spontaneously broken YM is given by
\alpha=\beta
. In this case, the coupling is clearly minimal. If
s=-1
, then the Lie algebra is the non-compact
\mathfrak{su}_{1,1}
, while the degenerate case with
s=0
(or equivalently,
\alpha\rightarrow 0
) corresponds to the non-semisimple
\mathfrak{e}_{2}
(the Euclidean algebra of the
2
d plane). The relative generator normalisations match onto the departure of the
W
-boson’s multipoles from minimal coupling. In this sense, the dipole moments encode the geometric “phase” of the theory. Incidentally, if the possible
F^{3}
interactions are neglected, then any departure of the amplitude (
247
) from minimal coupling has a Lie algebra interpretation. The partial unitarisation of the four vector boson amplitudes is therefore automatic.

The weak boson amplitudes match onto terms in an effective Lagrangian of anomalous cubic couplings. The multipole expansion applied here is (somewhat) traditionally parameterised as

\displaystyle A\left(W,\overline{W},\gamma^{+}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{e}{mx}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]^{2}-\frac{2(\mu+id)}{m}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left[3\bf{1}\bf\right]\left[3\bf{2}\bf\right]+\frac{(Q+i\widetilde{Q})}{m}x\left[3\bf{1}\bf\right]^{2}\left[3\bf{2}\bf\right]^{2},

(248)

where
e
is the electric charge,
\mu
and
d
are the magnetic and electric dipole moments respectively and
Q
and
\widetilde{Q}
are electric and magnetic quadrupole moments. Both
d
and
\widetilde{Q}
are
P
and
T
-violating interactions.

Off-shell, each of these couplings can be matched onto effective operators. A suitable basis of terms in the effective Lagrangian is
83

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{W\text{multi}}

\displaystyle=ie\kappa W^{+}_{\mu}W^{-}_{\nu}F^{\mu\nu}+ie\tilde{\kappa}W^{+}_{\mu}W^{-}_{\nu}\widetilde{F}^{\mu\nu}+i\frac{e\lambda}{m^{2}}W^{+\rho}_{\mu}W^{-\mu}_{\nu}F^{\,\,\nu}_{\rho}+i\frac{e\tilde{\lambda}}{m^{2}}W^{+\rho}_{\mu}W^{-\mu}_{\nu}\widetilde{F}^{\,\,\nu}_{\rho}

(249)

defining
W^{+}_{\mu\nu}=D_{\mu}W^{+}_{\nu}-D_{\nu}W^{+}_{\mu}
,
W^{-}_{\mu\nu}=D^{\dagger}_{\mu}W^{-}_{\nu}-D^{\dagger}_{\nu}W^{-}_{\mu}
and otherwise following the conventions and definitions of
106
. For brevity, I omit the standard kinetic terms that, by themselves, would otherwise describe regular
\mathfrak{su}_{2}\rightarrow\mathfrak{u}_{1}
spontaneously broken YM under which the
W
-bosons have electric charge
e
. Of course, this effective Lagrangian should be supplemented with appropriate quartic terms, but these do not affect the matching onto the
3
-particle amplitude, which is the meager goal here.

The operators in (
249
) can be evaluated for on-shell particles. The YM field and its Hodge dual become

\displaystyle F

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left[\bf{p}\bf\right\rvert S_{L}\left\lvert\bf{p}\bf\right]-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left\langle\bf{p}\right\rvert S_{R}\left\lvert\bf{p}\right\rangle

\displaystyle*F

\displaystyle=\frac{i}{\sqrt{2}}\left[\bf{p}\bf\right\rvert S_{L}\left\lvert\bf{p}\bf\right]+\frac{i}{\sqrt{2}}\left\langle\bf{p}\right\rvert S_{R}\left\lvert\bf{p}\right\rangle.

(250)

The first term is the self-dual component and the second is the anti-self-dual component:

\displaystyle G=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left[\bf{p}\bf\right\rvert S_{L}\left\lvert\bf{p}\bf\right]\qquad\qquad G^{\dagger}=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left\langle\bf{p}\right\rvert S_{R}\left\lvert\bf{p}\right\rangle.

(251)

The gauge connections
A
map directly onto polarisations:

\displaystyle\varepsilon=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}m}\left[\bf{p}\bf\right\rvert\sigma\left\lvert\bf{p}\right\rangle.

(252)

An extra factor of
\sqrt{2}
must be introduced for the longitudinally polarised mode to be correctly normalised, besides evaluating the suppressed spin indices at opposite values and symmetrising them.

Adding to these effective
3
-particle interactions the minimal coupling
-\frac{e}{mx}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle^{2}
term, the multipoles are matched onto as

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mu&=\frac{e}{2m}\left(2+\frac{1}{2}(\lambda-\kappa)\right)\\
Q&=\frac{e}{m^{2}}\left(-1+\frac{1}{2}\kappa\right)\end{split}\begin{split}d&=\frac{e}{4m}\left(\tilde{\lambda}-\tilde{\kappa}\right)\\
\widetilde{Q}&=\frac{e}{2m^{2}}\tilde{\kappa}\end{split}

(253)

I will use the word “anomalous” to mean departure from minimal coupling, which is represented by the constants
\kappa
,
\tilde{\kappa}
,
\lambda
and
\tilde{\lambda}
above. The significance of “minimal coupling” as defined in
9
is that it ultimately corresponds to the amplitude required for tree-level unitarity in the HEL. These definitions disagree with conventions established in previous studies of electroweak bosons
83
. Matching onto the specific gauged NL
\Sigma
M expression (
247
) corresponds to
e=\beta
,
\kappa=-(1-s\alpha/\beta)
and
\lambda=0
.

With
\mathcal{N}=1
supersymmetry, assuming that the massive vectors belong to massive vector multiplets, operators of the form
F^{3}
are prohibited, so
\lambda=\tilde{\lambda}=0
and
\kappa
and
\tilde{\kappa}
represent the only independent combination of anomalous multipoles (the quadrupole is effectively determined by the dipole).

In theories of supergravity with BPS vectors, the graviphoton commonly has a coupling of the form

\displaystyle A\left(W,\overline{W},\gamma^{+}\right)=\frac{m}{M_{Pl}}\frac{1}{mx}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle.

(254)

This corresponds to a large “anomalous” MDM with
g=1
and zero quadrupole (
g
being the gyromagnetic ratio). Matching onto (
247
), this theory of a single BPS vector (and its conjugate) in extended SUGRA is described by a non-compact
\mathfrak{su}_{1,1}
Lie algebra
59
. Because the coefficient, or electric charge, is
m/M_{Pl}
, the minimal coupling term vanishes in the massless limit, while the anomalous dipole term converges to a gravitational-strength effective interaction between same-helicity vectors and a scalar (arising from the longitudinal mode). This is the typical interaction involving the graviphoton that is expected for massless matter in extended SUGRA. See
109
for review and further details. This is an example of a theory that “ungauges” in the massless limit (which coincides here to switching off gravity), rather than converging to massless non-Abelian Yang-Mills. The cut-off of the underlying gauged NL
\Sigma
M is pushed to the Planck length through the very weak gauge coupling.

Appendix D
Extra Amplitudes

In this Appendix, I compile contributions to the massive
4
-particle amplitudes in Sections
3
and
4
that are predominantly tangential to the main narrative. These mostly arise from insertions of
3
-particle amplitudes that, off-shell, correspond to effective operators of the form
F^{3}
,
\varphi F^{2}
and
\chi F\chi
(or their parity-violating counterparts, which I will include implicitly in reference to these labels). On-shell, these
3
-particle amplitudes are respectively given by the
h
-terms in (
4.1
), the
c
-terms in (
98
) and the
m
-terms in (
2.3
).

The contributions presented here will mostly be the uninteresting ones from the viewpoint of seeking constraints from high-energy unitarity. More precisely, these are the terms with high-energy dependence that can only be softened by the outright elimination or suppression of their couplings. There are nevertheless a few exceptional cases in which these insertions do participate non-trivially in constraints, invariably related to their covariantisation. For clarity of exposition, the constraints associated to both the
F^{3}
and
\chi F\chi
are derived in this Appendix, while the
\varphi F^{2}
case is discussed in the main body of the text in Sections
4.2
and
4.3.2
since it is inextricably tied to the structure of the
f
-type couplings in the three vector amplitude.

Additionally, the calculation of the single vector and three scalar amplitude will also be presented here. This case is less interesting for the purposes of deriving constraints from high-energy unitarity because, in the absence of
\varphi F^{2}
insertions, it is manifestly unitary upon construction. It may nevertheless have relevance to the reconstruction of the emergent scalar potential in the HEL and can have implications for perturbative unitarity.

D.1
Low spin amplitudes

In this Appendix, I collect the contributions to the amplitudes in Sections
3.3
and
3.4
mediated by the fermion dipole interactions in (
2.3
). Additionally, I also give the general expressions for the low spin amplitudes mediated by massive vector exchange.

The general massive scalar amplitude induced by massive vector exchange is

\displaystyle A\left(\varphi_{i},\varphi_{j},\varphi_{k},\varphi_{l}\right)=\frac{1}{s-m_{s_{M}}^{2}}(t_{M})_{ij}(t_{M})_{kl}\frac{1}{2m_{s_{M}}^{2}}\left(m_{s_{M}}^{2}\left(t-u\right)+\left(m_{1}^{2}-m_{2}^{2}\right)\left(m_{3}^{2}-m_{4}^{2}\right)\right).

(255)

The scalar QED amplitude (
79
) is (equivalent to) a special case of this (modulo the quartic contact term).

The general mixed fermion-scalar amplitude mediated by a massive vector is

\displaystyle A\left(\psi_{i},\psi_{j},\varphi_{k},\varphi_{l}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{s-m_{s_{M}}^{2}}\frac{1}{2}(t_{M})_{kl}\Bigg((t_{M})_{ij}\left(\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{3}-p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]+\frac{m_{3}^{2}-m_{4}^{2}}{m_{s_{M}}^{2}}\left(m_{1}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]-m_{2}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\right)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-(t_{M})_{ji}\left(\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert p_{3}-p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]-\frac{m_{3}^{2}-m_{4}^{2}}{m_{s_{M}}^{2}}\left(m_{2}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]-m_{1}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\right)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{2((m_{M})_{ij})^{*}}{\Lambda}\left[\bf{1}\bf\right\rvert p_{4}p_{3}-p_{3}p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]-\frac{2(m_{M})_{ij}}{\Lambda}\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{4}p_{3}-p_{3}p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{2}\right\rangle\Bigg).

(256)

Again, this clearly agrees with the QED amplitude (
90
).

The general
4
-fermion amplitude is given in (
3.4
) without inclusion of the dipole
m
-type couplings. The remaining terms are given by

\displaystyle A_{\chi F\chi}(\psi_{i},\psi_{j},\psi_{k},\psi_{l})

\displaystyle=\frac{-1}{s-{m_{s_{M}}^{2}}}\bigg(-\frac{2((m_{M})_{ij})^{*}(m_{M})_{kl}}{\Lambda^{2}}\left(\left[\bf{1}\bf\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{3}\right\rangle\left[\bf{2}\bf\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{4}\right\rangle+\left[\bf{1}\bf\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{4}\right\rangle\left[\bf{2}\bf\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{3}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad-\frac{2(m_{M})_{ij}((m_{M})_{kl})^{*}}{\Lambda^{2}}\left(\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{3}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{4}\bf\right]+\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{4}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{3}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{2(m_{M})_{ij}(m_{M})_{kl}}{\Lambda^{2}}m_{s_{M}}^{2}\left(\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle+\left\langle\bf{14}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{2((m_{M})_{ij}(m_{M})_{kl})^{*}}{\Lambda^{2}}m_{s_{M}}^{2}\left(\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]+\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+(t_{M})_{ij}\frac{((m_{M})_{kl})^{*}}{\Lambda}\left(\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left[\bf{4}\bf\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{1}\right\rangle+\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]\left[\bf{3}\bf\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{1}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+(t_{M})_{ji}\frac{(m_{M})_{kl}}{\Lambda}\left(\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{4}\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]+\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{3}\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-(t_{M})_{ij}\frac{(m_{M})_{kl}}{\Lambda}\left(\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{4}\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]+\left\langle\bf{14}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{3}\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-(t_{M})_{ji}\frac{((m_{M})_{kl})^{*}}{\Lambda}\left(\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{4}\bf\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{2}\right\rangle+\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\left[\bf{3}\bf\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{2}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+(t_{M})_{kl}\frac{((m_{M})_{ij})^{*}}{\Lambda}\left(\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\left[\bf{2}\bf\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{3}\right\rangle+\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]\left[\bf{1}\bf\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{3}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+(t_{M})_{lk}\frac{(m_{M})_{ij}}{\Lambda}\left(\left\langle\bf{14}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{3}\bf\right]+\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{3}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-(t_{M})_{kl}\frac{(m_{M})_{ij}}{\Lambda}\left(\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{4}\bf\right]+\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{3}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-(t_{M})_{lk}\frac{((m_{M})_{ij})^{*}}{\Lambda}\left(\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{2}\bf\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{4}\right\rangle+\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left[\bf{1}\bf\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{3}\right\rangle\right)\bigg).

(257)

I explicitly state parity conjugate structures here simply because I do in (
3.4
). As in the main text,
P_{s}=p_{3}+p_{4}
.

Clearly, these diverge as
\sim E^{2}
for the
(+,+,-,-)
helicity configuration (and its parity conjugate). For this amplitude, the lowest dimension contact terms also share this degree of high energy dependence, so should generally be included at this order. However, they cannot cancel the leading HEL divergence from the factorisation terms constructed above. This is clear from converting to the
\{\mathbf{L}_{i}\}
basis using (
4.2.1
). The leading
\sim E^{2}
terms are then of the form
\frac{(u-t)}{s-m_{s_{M}}^{2}}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{34}\right\rangle\rightarrow\frac{(u-t)}{s}\left[12\bf\right]\left\langle 34\right\rangle
, which clearly retains the pole in the HEL and therefore cannot be canceled by contact terms. It is also clear that these divergent terms cannot be canceled by other terms in different channels, since the divergent helicity configurations in the other channels are distinct (they correspond to different pairings of the same-helicity fermions). The only way for them to be removed is by eliminating the dipole interactions.

D.2
Four vectors

The
F^{3}
-type
3
-particle couplings induce contributions to the four vector amplitude. The double insertion terms are given by

\displaystyle A_{sF^{3}F^{3}}\left(W_{A},W_{B},W_{C},W_{D}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{-1}{s-m_{s_{M}^{2}}}\frac{1}{2\Lambda^{4}}\bigg(h_{ABM}(h_{MCD})^{*}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{34}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\bigg(\left(u-t\right)\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{34}\right\rangle+\frac{1}{6}\left(\frac{m_{2}}{m_{1}}\hat{\mathbf{L}}_{1}+\frac{m_{1}}{m_{2}}\hat{\mathbf{L}}_{2}+\frac{m_{4}}{m_{3}}\mathbf{L}_{3}+\frac{m_{3}}{m_{4}}\mathbf{L}_{4}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{2}{3}m_{1}m_{2}\left(\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle+\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{14}\right\rangle\right)+\frac{2}{3}m_{3}m_{4}\left(\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]+\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\right)\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+h_{ABM}h_{MCD}m_{s_{M}}^{2}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left[\bf{34}\bf\right]\left(\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]+\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\right)\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\text{Parity conj.}

(258)

The first Lorentz structure on the right-hand side has been converted into a form with transparent HEL scaling through application of (
4.2.1
). The leading HEL divergence clearly scales as
\sim E^{4}
for the MHV helicity configuration and is given by the term proportional to
u-t
. This can’t be canceled by contact terms because the Lorentz structure contains a pole (and agrees with the expected massless amplitude containing a pole), nor can it be canceled by terms from other channels. As long as the
h
-type couplings are present, these terms are present.

The single insertion terms are given by

\displaystyle A_{sF^{3}}\left(W_{A},W_{B},W_{C},W_{D}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{-1}{s-m_{s_{M}^{2}}}\frac{1}{4\Lambda^{2}}\frac{1}{m_{3}m_{4}}h_{ABM}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\times\Bigg((f_{CD}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M})^{*}\bigg(\left[\bf{34}\bf\right]\bigg(\frac{1}{6}\left(\frac{m_{4}}{m_{3}}\mathbf{L}_{3}+\frac{m_{3}}{m_{4}}\mathbf{L}_{4}\right)+\frac{4}{3}m_{1}m_{2}\left(\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle+\left\langle\bf{14}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{2}{3}m_{3}m_{4}\left(\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]+\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\right)\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\frac{1}{6}\left\langle\bf{34}\right\rangle\left(\mathbf{L}_{3}+\mathbf{L}_{4}\right)-\left(m_{3}\left[\bf{34}\bf\right]-m_{4}\left\langle\bf{34}\right\rangle\right)\left(m_{1}\left\langle\bf{14}\right\rangle\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]+m_{2}\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\left(m_{4}\left[\bf{34}\bf\right]-m_{3}\left\langle\bf{34}\right\rangle\right)\left(m_{1}\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]+m_{2}\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\left(m_{s_{M}}^{2}-\frac{1}{3}\left(m_{3}^{2}+m_{4}^{2}+2m_{1}^{2}+2m_{2}^{2}\right)\right)\left\langle\bf{34}\right\rangle\left(\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]+\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\right)\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+f_{CD}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M}m_{s_{M}}^{2}\left\langle\bf{34}\right\rangle\left(\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]+\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\left(f_{DM}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,C}m_{3}\left[\bf{34}\bf\right]+(f_{MC}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,D})^{*}m_{4}\left\langle\bf{34}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\left(\frac{1}{6m_{4}}\mathbf{L}_{4}+m_{1}\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{14}\right\rangle+m_{2}\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle+\frac{1}{3}m_{4}\left(\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]+\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\right)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\left(f_{MC}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,D}m_{4}\left[\bf{34}\bf\right]+(f_{DM}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,C})^{*}m_{3}\left\langle\bf{34}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\left(\frac{1}{6m_{3}}\mathbf{L}_{3}+m_{1}\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle+m_{2}\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle+\frac{1}{3}m_{3}\left(\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]+\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\right)\right)\Bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\text{Parity conj.}.

(259)

To this expression, terms generated by the simultaneous exchanges
1\leftrightarrow 3
and
2\leftrightarrow 4
should be added. This amplitude has been converted to the
\{\mathbf{L}_{i}\}
basis using (
4.2.1
), as well as identity (
4.2.1
) again. The
t
and
u
-channel expressions are inferable from exchanges. This time, contact interactions do have to be considered and tuned against the leading HEL divergent terms. This has been implicitly performed in the expression above so that the leading HEL scaling arises at
\sim E^{3}
. Finally, the identity (
A
) has also been applied to convert all terms containing factors of
\mathbf{L}_{i}
or
\hat{\mathbf{L}}_{i}
into a linearly independent basis. I choose this basis to consist of Lorentz structures in which the spinor bilinear prefactors accompanying the factors of
\mathbf{L}_{i}
are as holomorphic as possible.

The amplitude’s terms (
D.2
) presented above that are generated by
F^{3}
insertions have leading order HEL scaling
\sim E^{3}
after tuning by contact terms. This occurs for two classes of helicity configurations. The first consists of one longitudinal and three transverse helicities, of which one is opposite the other two. This combination receives contributions from the double insertion terms in (
D.2
), both as the leading order limit of the terms proportional to
\mathbf{L}_{i}
and
\hat{\mathbf{L}}_{i}
, and as subleading limits of the
\sim E^{4}
terms. Because there are contributions from subleading terms, these limits contain residual reference spinors (“gauge artifacts”).

The single
F^{3}
insertion terms that scale with this configuration are expected to match onto massless amplitudes generated by massless
F^{3}
couplings and the emergent
\varphi F^{2}
couplings contained within (
4.1
). This can be verified for e.g. the
(++,++,--,L)
configuration, where the
s
-channel terms converge to

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{m_{4}}h_{ABM}\left((f_{DM}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,C})^{*}-(f_{CD}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M})^{*}\right)\frac{\left[12\bf\right]^{2}\left[14\bf\right]\left\langle 13\right\rangle\left\langle 34\right\rangle}{s}

(260)

This still contains the
s
-pole, so is linearly independent of the other channels. Identifying (
4.1
), these terms have the expected structure of the massless amplitude mediated by both dim-
6
and dim-
5
insertions. They cannot be reduced without the elimination of (at least one of) these couplings.

The second class of leading helicity configurations consists of three transverse helicities of the same sign and one longitudinal helicity. In these cases, the amplitude converges to contact terms which can interfere between channels. For example, the
(++,++,++,L)
configuration terms converge to the Lorentz structure
\left[12\bf\right]\left[13\bf\right]\left[23\bf\right]
. Requiring that this cancels gives the condition

\displaystyle\left(f_{MD}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,C}+(f_{DC}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M})^{*}\right)h_{ABM}+\left(f_{MD}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,B}+(f_{DB}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M})^{*}\right)h_{AMC}+\left(f_{MD}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,A}+(f_{DA}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M})^{*}\right)h_{MBC}=0.

(261)

This is close to the expected covariance condition on the
h_{ABC}
couplings, but differs by contaminant terms proportional to
c_{CM}^{D}h_{ABM}
(and others related by index permutations). This is similar to the other covariance conditions on the
c
-type and
m
-type couplings derived in Section
4.3.2
and Appendix
D.6
below. When both the
c_{CM}^{D}
and
h_{ABC}
couplings are suppressed compared to the terms in the full amplitude proportional to the
f
-couplings, the contaminant terms are a small correction.

When one of the gluons is massless (choose
D
in (
261
)), then (
113
) ensures that the relation becomes exactly the covariance of the
h
-couplings. This is enforced by consistent factorisation and is not an optional property that could emerge from high energy unitarity.

It remains to explicitly perform the cancellations facilitated by (
261
). The terms contributing to the limit are given by

\displaystyle\frac{-1}{24}\frac{1}{m_{4}^{2}\Lambda^{2}}\mathbf{L}_{4}\Bigg(

\displaystyle\left(f_{DM}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,C}+(f_{CD}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M})^{*}\right)h_{MAB}\frac{\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left[\bf{34}\bf\right]}{s-m_{s_{M}}^{2}}+\left(f_{DM}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,B}+(f_{BD}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M})^{*}\right)h_{MAC}\frac{\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]}{t-m_{t_{M}}^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+\left(f_{DM}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,A}+(f_{AD}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M})^{*}\right)h_{MCB}\frac{\left[\bf{32}\bf\right]\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]}{u-m_{u_{M}}^{2}}\Bigg).

(262)

The cancellation clearly proceeds through another application of (
4.2
), although I will not bother to explicitly present this.

Notably, none of the divergent
\sim E^{3}
terms in the amplitudes presented here arise for the three longitudinal, one transverse helicity configuration for which the amplitude constructed from
f
-type couplings in Section
4.2.1
diverges. The presence of the
h
-type couplings therefore does not modify any of the conclusions presented there.

In summary, the
F^{3}
couplings are responsible for the strongest high energy dependence of the four vector amplitude in the absence of
4
-particle contact terms. The amplitude scales like
A\sim(hE^{2}/\Lambda^{2})^{2}
as a result. The only way in which they can viably admit an extended energy range far beyond the particle masses (
E\gg m
) is if
hE^{2}/\Lambda\ll\sqrt{4\pi}
. Having suppressed the
\sim E^{4}
terms, the
\sim E^{3}
terms become leading. There are three such classes of terms: the two mentioned above with single
F^{3}
insertions and the class discussed in Section
4.2.1
involving only the
f
-type couplings. As explained above, suppression of the terms with only
f
-type couplings can be arranged non-trivially by demanding that they obey the Jacobi and GCS constraints. The other two classes of terms contain single insertions of
h/\Lambda^{2}
, so can be made comparatively small. However, as is clear in (
D.2
), there are single insertion terms of the form
\frac{h}{\Lambda^{2}}\frac{f}{m}E^{3}
and
\frac{h}{\Lambda^{2}}fE^{2}
and terms of the latter form cannot be canceled in the HEL. The relation (
261
) must be satisfied in order for the
E^{3}
terms not to dominate over the weaker
E^{2}
at high enough energies. Covariance emerges in the further limit that
\frac{cE}{\Lambda}
are also small deformations, which is also required for the associated suppression of the
\sim E^{3}
terms described around (
260
).

D.3
Two vectors and two scalars

This Appendix compiles the contributions to the two vector, two scalar amplitude from Section
4.3.1
induced by insertions of
F^{3}
and
\varphi F^{2}
. The
s
-channel scalar exchange is modified to include

\displaystyle A_{s\varphi F^{2}}(W_{A},W_{B},\varphi_{i},\varphi_{j})=\frac{-1}{s-m_{s_{m}}^{2}}C_{ijm}\left(\frac{c^{m}_{AB}}{\Lambda}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]^{2}+\frac{(c^{m}_{AB})^{*}}{\Lambda}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle^{2}\right),

(263)

which is manifestly unitary in the HEL, while the vector exchange must also include

\displaystyle A_{sF^{3}}(W_{A},W_{B},\varphi_{i},\varphi_{j})

\displaystyle=\frac{-1}{s-m_{s_{M}}^{2}}\frac{i}{2}(t_{M})_{ij}\bigg(\frac{h_{ABM}}{\Lambda^{2}}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left[\bf{1}\bf\right\rvert p_{3}p_{4}-p_{4}p_{3}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{(h_{ABM})^{*}}{\Lambda^{2}}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert p_{3}p_{4}-p_{4}p_{3}\left\lvert\bf{2}\right\rangle\bigg).

(264)

The
t
-channel vector exchange amplitude is extended to include

\displaystyle A_{t\varphi F^{2}}(W_{A},W_{B},\varphi_{i},\varphi_{j})

\displaystyle=\frac{-1}{t-m_{t_{M}}^{2}}\Bigg(\frac{c^{i}_{AM}(c^{j}_{BM})^{*}}{\Lambda^{2}}\left[\bf{1}\bf\right\rvert P_{t}\left\lvert\bf{2}\right\rangle^{2}+\frac{(c^{i}_{AM})^{*}c^{j}_{BM}}{\Lambda^{2}}\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert P_{t}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{c^{i}_{AM}c^{j}_{BM}}{\Lambda^{2}}m_{t_{M}}^{2}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]^{2}+\frac{(c^{i}_{AM}c^{j}_{BM})^{*}}{\Lambda^{2}}m_{t_{M}}^{2}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad-\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2}}m_{t_{M}}\Bigg(\lambda^{i}_{AM}\left(\frac{c^{j}_{BM}}{\Lambda}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left[\bf{2}\bf\right\rvert P_{t}\left\lvert\bf{1}\right\rangle+\frac{(c^{j}_{BM})^{*}}{\Lambda}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert P_{t}\left\lvert\bf{1}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\lambda^{j}_{BM}\left(\frac{c^{i}_{AM}}{\Lambda}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left[\bf{1}\bf\right\rvert P_{t}\left\lvert\bf{2}\right\rangle+\frac{(c^{i}_{AM})^{*}}{\Lambda}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert P_{t}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]\right)\Bigg)\Bigg)

(265)

(I omit the contribution induced purely from the Higgs interactions described already in Section
4.3.1
). The
u
-channel expressions can be inferred by exchanging
1\leftrightarrow 2
.

Clearly these terms have
\sim E^{2}
HEL scaling. This occurs for same sign transverse helicities in (
D.3
) and opposite sign transverse helicities in (
D.3
). In the case of the former, the divergence cannot be canceled by a contact term because the massless Lorentz structure to which the amplitude converges to still contains a pole. It can only be removed if
h_{ABM}(t_{M})_{ij}=0
, meaning that, in some basis, the vectors coupling to the scalars (at least through (
12
)) cannot participate in
F^{3}
interactions. In the latter case, the contributing terms in the
t
and
u
-channels cannot cancel in the massless limit because the presence of uncanceled
t
and
u
poles ensures that they remain linearly independent. These terms can only be removed by eliminating the
c
-type couplings. None of these terms interfere with the limits analysed in Section
4.3.1
.

D.4
Three vectors and one scalar

I give here the expressions for the amplitudes induced by insertion of the
F^{3}
terms. These are

\displaystyle A_{F^{3}}\left(W_{A},W_{B},W_{C},\varphi_{i}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{s-m_{s_{M}}^{2}}\frac{i}{\Lambda^{2}}h_{ABM}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\times\bigg(\frac{c^{i}_{MC}}{\Lambda}m_{s_{M}}^{2}\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]+\frac{(c^{i}_{MC})^{*}}{\Lambda}\left(\left[\bf{1}\bf\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{3}\right\rangle-m_{3}\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\right)\left(\left[\bf{2}\bf\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{3}\right\rangle-m_{3}\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2}}m_{s_{M}}\lambda^{i}_{MC}\left(\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{2}\bf\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{3}\right\rangle+\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left[\bf{1}\bf\right\rvert p_{4}\left\lvert\bf{3}\right\rangle-2m_{3}\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\right)\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\text{Parity conj.},

(266)

where the
P
-conjugate terms are given by switching bracket shapes and complex conjugating the accompanying couplings. As usual, the
t
-channel terms are obtained by exchanging
2\leftrightarrow 3
and the
u
-channel from exchanging
1\leftrightarrow 3
. The single insertion terms of
\varphi F^{2}
without
F^{3}
are covered in Section
4.3.2
.

The leading order high energy dependence is given by helicity configurations of the form
(++,++,--)
. This only receives contributions from the
h_{ABM}(c_{MC}^{i})^{*}
term, is
\sim E^{3}
and cannot be canceled by a contact term (because of the presence of a pole). Switching off the
\varphi F^{2}
couplings, the remaining leading divergence is
\sim E^{2}
and arises for configurations like
(++,++,L)
. While this is the same configuration for which leading transverse divergences appeared in (
4.3.2
), the Lorentz structures are linearly independent. The conditions obtained by demanding the softening of these terms therefore do not interfere with each other. This leads to the requirement that
h_{ABM}(t_{M})_{Ci}=0
and therefore that the vectors participating in
F^{3}
interactions cannot couple to scalars.

D.5
One vector, two fermions and one scalar

Here is the collection of contributions to the mixed amplitude in Section
4.4.1
involving insertions of
\varphi F^{2}
and
\chi F\chi
. The
t
-channel is modified to include

\displaystyle A_{t\chi F\chi}\left(\varphi_{m},W_{A},\psi_{i},\psi_{j}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{-2}{t-m_{t_{k}}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\bigg(y^{m}_{ik}\frac{((m_{A})_{kj})^{*}}{\Lambda}\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{3}\right\rvert P_{t}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]-(y^{m}_{ik})^{*}\frac{(m_{A})_{kj}}{\Lambda}\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert P_{t}\left\lvert\bf{3}\bf\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+(y^{m}_{ik})^{*}\frac{((m_{A})_{kj})^{*}}{\Lambda}m_{t_{k}}\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]-y^{m}_{ik}\frac{(m_{A})_{kj}}{\Lambda}m_{t_{k}}\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle\bigg).

(267)

The
u
-channel can be inferred from exchanging the fermions. The
s
-channel vector exchange terms are modified to include

\displaystyle A_{s\{\varphi F^{2},\chi F\chi\}}\left(\varphi_{m},W_{A},\psi_{i},\psi_{j}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{-1}{s-m_{s_{M}}^{2}}\bigg(\frac{c^{m}_{AM}}{\Lambda}\frac{2(m_{M})_{ji}}{\Lambda}\left[\bf{2}\bf\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{3}\right\rangle\left[\bf{2}\bf\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{4}\right\rangle+\frac{(c^{m}_{AM})^{*}}{\Lambda}\frac{2((m_{M})_{ij})^{*}}{\Lambda}\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{3}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{4}\bf\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{c^{m}_{AM}}{\Lambda}\frac{2((m_{M})_{ij})^{*}}{\Lambda}m_{s_{M}}^{2}\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]+\frac{(c^{m}_{AM})^{*}}{\Lambda}\frac{2(m_{M})_{ji}}{\Lambda}m_{s_{M}}^{2}\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{c_{AM}^{m}}{\Lambda}(t_{M})_{ij}\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]\left[\bf{2}\bf\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{3}\right\rangle-\frac{c_{AM}^{m}}{\Lambda}((t_{M})_{ij})^{*}\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left[\bf{2}\bf\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{4}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{(c_{AM}^{m})^{*}}{\Lambda}(t_{M})_{ij}\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{4}\bf\right]-\frac{(c_{AM}^{m})^{*}}{\Lambda}((t_{M})_{ij})^{*}\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{3}\bf\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{m_{s_{M}}}{\sqrt{2}}\lambda_{AM}^{m}\bigg(\frac{((m_{M})_{ji})^{*}}{\Lambda}\left(\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{4}\bf\right]+\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{3}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{(m_{M})_{ij}}{\Lambda}\left(\left\langle\bf{23}\right\rangle\left[\bf{2}\bf\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{4}\right\rangle+\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle\left[\bf{2}\bf\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{3}\right\rangle\right)\bigg)\bigg).

(268)

This includes single and double insertions of “higher-dimensional operators”.

The
s
-channel double insertion terms (those with both
\varphi F^{2}
and
\chi F\chi
) scale as
\sim E^{2}
in the HEL for the
(++,-,-)
configuration and its parity conjugate. This can only be eliminated by eliminating the associated combination of couplings (it cannot be canceled by a contact term because the Lorentz structure still contains a pole in the HEL). At
\sim E
, the
s
-channel single insertions of fermion dipoles contribute for the same helicity configurations (
(L,+,+)
and conjugate) as the leading divergences of the amplitude presented in (
4.4.1
). However, the terms in (
D.5
) converge to linearly independent Lorentz structures containing a pole, whereas the terms in (
4.4.1
) converge to contact amplitudes. The limits of these terms therefore do not interfere with each other. Finally, the expressions above also include
\sim E
divergences for the helicity configurations
(++,-,+)
and
(++,+,-)
, as well as their parity conjugates. These are generated by the
\varphi F^{2}
couplings in the
s
-channel and the dipole couplings in the
t
and
u
-channels. However, the terms emerging in the HEL in each channel retain their poles and remain linearly independent, so cannot cancel each other. Again, the only way to eliminate these divergences is by precluding the accompanying combination of couplings. This all amounts to a forbidding of vectors or fermions involved in the
\varphi F^{2}
or
\chi F\chi
interactions (in some basis, possibly not the mass eigenbasis) from coupling either to each other or through the other matter couplings (
y_{ij}^{m}
,
(t_{M})_{ij}
and
\lambda_{AM}^{m}
) involved in this amplitude.

D.6
Two vectors and two fermions

This Appendix begins with the collection of terms in the massive vector and fermion amplitude from Section
4.4.2
that are induced by
F^{3}
interactions. The contributions purely from the dipole moments of the fermions are given further below, while the
\varphi F^{2}
interactions are covered in Section
4.4.2
. With the inclusion of
F^{3}
, only the
s
-channel is modified:

\displaystyle A_{sF^{3}}\left(W_{A},W_{B},\psi_{i},\psi_{j}\right)

(269)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\frac{-1}{s-m_{s_{M}}^{2}}\frac{i}{2\Lambda^{2}}h_{ABM}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\bigg(\frac{2(m_{M})_{ij}}{\Lambda}\left(\left[\bf{2}\bf\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{3}\right\rangle\left[\bf{1}\bf\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{4}\right\rangle+\left[\bf{2}\bf\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{4}\right\rangle\left[\bf{1}\bf\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{3}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{2((m_{M})_{ji})^{*}}{\Lambda}m_{s_{M}}^{2}\left(\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]+\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-(t_{M})_{ij}\left(\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]\left[\bf{1}\bf\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{3}\right\rangle+\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\left[\bf{2}\bf\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{3}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+((t_{M})_{ij})^{*}\left(\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left[\bf{1}\bf\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{4}\right\rangle+\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{2}\bf\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{4}\right\rangle\right)\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\text{Parity conj.}

(270)

The leading HEL scaling is
\sim E^{3}
for the
(++,++,-,-)
helicity configuration (and conjugate). This is proportional to
h_{ABM}(m_{M})_{ij}
and can only be removed by direct elimination of this factor (again, the massless Lorentz structure that these terms match onto contains a pole and cannot be canceled by a contact term). Doing so leaves behind
\sim E^{2}
divergent terms for
(++,++,-,+)
and
(++,++,+,-)
configurations, as well as their conjugates, which are respectively proportional to
h_{ABM}(t_{M})_{ij}
,
h_{ABM}((t_{M})_{ij})^{*}
and their conjugates. These configurations are distinct from those that yielded the Lie algebra commutation relations for the
(t_{M})_{ij}
couplings, as well as those induced by the dipole moment insertions, so don’t interfere with the arguments in Section
4.4.2
and below. They can only be eliminated by eliminating the associated coupling constants, necessitating that, in an appropriate basis (possibly not the mass eigenbasis), the vector bosons with
F^{3}
interactions cannot couple to the fermions.

I next present separately the terms induced from insertions of the fermion dipole interactions (the
m
-type couplings in (
2.3
)). They are given by

\displaystyle A_{t\chi F\chi}\left(W_{A},W_{B},\psi_{i},\psi_{j}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{-1}{t-m_{t_{k}}^{2}}\Bigg(\frac{4((m_{A})_{ik})^{*}(m_{B})_{jk}}{\Lambda^{2}}\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle\left[\bf{1}\bf\right\rvert P_{t}\left\lvert\bf{2}\right\rangle-\frac{4((m_{A})_{ik}(m_{B})_{kj})^{*}}{\Lambda^{2}}m_{t_{k}}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{2((m_{A})_{ik})^{*}}{\Lambda}\frac{1}{m_{2}}(t_{B})_{kj}\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]\left[\bf{1}\bf\right\rvert P_{t}\left\lvert\bf{2}\right\rangle+\frac{2((m_{B})_{kj})^{*}}{\Lambda}\frac{1}{m_{1}}((t_{A})_{ik})^{*}\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert P_{t}\left\lvert\bf{2}\bf\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\frac{2((m_{A})_{ik})^{*}}{\Lambda}((t_{B})_{kj})^{*}\frac{m_{t_{k}}}{m_{2}}\left\langle\bf{24}\right\rangle\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]+\frac{2((m_{B})_{kj})^{*}}{\Lambda}(t_{A})_{ik}\frac{m_{t_{k}}}{m_{1}}\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{13}\right\rangle\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]\Bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\text{Parity conj.}

(271)

(with
u
-channel obtained by swapping the vectors) and

\displaystyle A_{s\chi F\chi}\left(W_{A},W_{B},\psi_{i},\psi_{j}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{-1}{s-m_{s_{M}}^{2}}\frac{i}{2m_{1}m_{2}}\frac{((m_{M})_{ij})^{*}}{\Lambda}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\Bigg(\left(\left({f}_{BM}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,A}+({f}_{AB}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M})^{*}\right)m_{1}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]+\left(({f}_{MA}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,B})^{*}-({f}_{AB}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M})^{*}\right)m_{2}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\left(\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{4}\bf\right]+\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{2}\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{3}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\left(\left({f}_{MA}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,B}+({f}_{AB}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M})^{*}\right)m_{2}\left[\bf{12}\bf\right]+\left(({f}_{BM}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,A})^{*}-({f}_{AB}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M})^{*}\right)m_{1}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\left(\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{4}\bf\right]+\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]\left\langle\bf{1}\right\rvert P_{s}\left\lvert\bf{3}\bf\right]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\left({f}_{AB}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M}+({f}_{AB}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M})^{*}\right)m_{s_{M}}^{2}\left\langle\bf{12}\right\rangle\left(\left[\bf{13}\bf\right]\left[\bf{24}\bf\right]+\left[\bf{23}\bf\right]\left[\bf{14}\bf\right]\right)\Bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\text{Parity conj.}

(272)

The parity conjugate terms are given, as usual, by swapping the bracket shapes and complex conjugating the coefficients (I omit writing them explicitly for brevity).

The double insertion terms of the dipoles scale as
\sim E^{2}
for the
(++,--,+,-)
helicity configuration (and its conjugate). All contact terms have scaling
\mathcal{O}(E^{3})
, so cannot be involved in reducing the HEL. The divergent terms can only be removed by eliminating the dipole couplings, which is consistent with off-shell expectations. The terms with single insertions also scale as
\sim E^{2}
, this time for helicity configurations with one longitudinal and one transverse vector. The configuration
(L,++,-,-)
only receives contributions from
s
-channel terms proportional to
\frac{1}{m_{1}}\left({f}_{MA}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,B}-{f}_{AB}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M}\right)(m_{M})_{ij}\propto c_{BM}^{A}(m_{M})_{ij}
, (the configuration
(--,L,+,+)
and both of their parity conjugates give similar results). This is the expected dependence arising from insertions of both a fermion dipole and an emergent
\varphi F^{2}
operator in the HEL in which the scalar is identified with the longitudinal vector. The divergence for these configurations can only be reduced by removing
c_{BM}^{A}
(equivalently (
148
)) or the dipole couplings.

The divergences for the configurations
(--,L,-,-)
(and those related by parity and exchanges) are more intricate and receive contributions from all three factorisation channels. This cancellation requires

\displaystyle(t_{B})_{ik}(m_{A})_{kj}+(t_{B})_{jk}(m_{A})_{ik}+\frac{i}{2}\left(({f}_{MA}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,B})^{*}+{f}_{AB}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M}\right)(m_{M})_{ij}=0,

(273)

which is almost the expected expression for covariance of the dipole interactions under the Lie algebra. The covariance relation is contaminated by the presence of
({f}_{MA}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,B}-{f}_{AB}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M})^{*}(m_{M})_{ij}\propto m_{1}(c^{A}_{BM})^{*}(m_{M})_{ij}
, in spite of the fact that the emergent
\varphi F^{2}
interaction is not expected to mediate the massless amplitude upon which this helicity configuration should match onto. This is similar to the previous cases of the near covariance of the
F^{3}
and
\varphi F^{2}
interactions explained in Sections
4.2
,
4.3.2
and
D.2
. Just as for those cases, the relation (
273
) is only significant when the emergent contact term that these terms converge to otherwise carries the leading order energy dependence. The cancellation of this term provided by (
273
) ensures that the cut-off of the theory is extended as far as possible while allowing for these effective interactions to exist. If all of the conditions required for high energy unitarity are otherwise met and small dim-
5
deformations are additionally introduced, then (
273
) is required to remove what would otherwise be the single leading class of terms in the HEL obstructing perturbative unitarity (with a cut-off
\sim\sqrt{m\Lambda}
instead of
\Lambda
).

Note that, if vector
A
is massless, then (
113
) ensures that (
273
) becomes the expected uncontaminated covariance relation. Furthermore, this constraint becomes necessitated by consistent factorisation, which can be demonstrated by a similar argument to that sketched-out above for demonstrating covariance of other higher-dim operator insertions described in previous Sections under analogous conditions.

It remains to convert the residues in (
D.6
) and (
D.6
) into a form in which the cancellation for the
(--,L,-,-)
configuration (and its partners) is made manifest. This simply involves another application of (
4.2
) and I will not bother to present its implementation here. I will, however, mention that the residual terms have leading
\sim E
HEL divergences for helicity configurations in which both vectors are transverse and both fermion helicities are the same. These do not interfere with any of the
\sim E
divergent terms encountered in the analysis in Section
4.4.2
, which all arise for different helicity configurations.

D.7
One vector and three scalars

The final amplitude to complete the catalogue of tree-level
4
-particle massive amplitudes of particles of spin
\leq 1
is
A(\varphi_{i},\varphi_{j},\varphi_{k},W_{A})
. All three factorisation channels for this amplitude are again related by exchange symmetries, so only the
s
-channel need be explicitly calculated.
